# Xyst development build status — 2026-10-28

## MCP list-result schema conformance — 2026-10-28

- MCP tool, resource, resource-template, and prompt list responses validate required arrays and definition fields, including JSON Schema object input schemas, optional prompt arguments, pagination cursors, annotations, icons, and resource metadata. Discovery validates known capability flags/maps and optional instructions; resource text/blob alternative fields are type-checked. `tools/call.structuredContent` accepts any JSON value (including arrays, scalars, and `null`) as the official schema specifies.
- Focused protocol and MCP transport/integration suites pass 36 tests with one expected Linux sandbox capability skip; changed-file Biome checks pass. Hosted CI for this source is pending. Reference: [official MCP 2026-07-28 JSON schema](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/blob/main/schema/2026-07-28/schema.json).

## MCP result-shape conformance — 2026-10-28

- Modern discovery, tool, resource, and prompt method responses now require the schema's `resultType`; cacheable responses require valid cache scope and nonnegative integer TTL; discovery validates version and capability containers; resource reads validate text/blob records; prompt responses validate message roles and content blocks. Unsupported control flow remains fail closed; legacy responses keep omitted-field compatibility.
- Focused protocol validation passes 5/5; stdio and Streamable HTTP integration regressions pass 2/2; focused API, signed-package, and MCP package API fixtures pass 3/3; changed-file Biome checks pass. Hosted run [40614322437](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent/actions/runs/40614322437) confirms the cache/shape validation on exact source; security and both desktop package lifecycle jobs pass. Windows Node 22/24 each passed 937/959 with 21 skips and the known ConPTY resize failure; Ubuntu Node 22/24 each passed 951/959 with six skips and the known Bubblewrap loopback plus dependent signed-MCP isolation failures. Full MCP field-level schema validation remains open. Reference: [official MCP 2026-07-28 JSON schema](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/blob/main/2026-07-28/schema.json).

## Windows atomic-replacement cleanup warning — 2026-10-28

- If Windows installs a replacement but cannot remove the old-file backup, `atomicWriteFile` treats the write as successful, preserves the recovery backup, and reports a persistence warning. Session writes forward that warning while advancing their in-memory revision, so callers do not report failure after the new data is already visible.
- The focused fault-injection regression passes (1/1); Biome and whitespace checks pass. Hosted run [40614322437](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent/actions/runs/40614322437) verifies this behavior on the exact source; security and both desktop package lifecycle jobs pass. Only the previously documented ConPTY and Bubblewrap test-runner limitations remain in the Node matrices.

## Bounded tool result transport — 2026-10-27

- Built-in JSON tool responses now have a hard 1 MB runtime ceiling, including unrestricted mode. File reads, edits, and writes are capped at 32 MiB; directory iteration streams names, checks cancellation, scans at most 50,000 names, and returns at most 5,000 entries, avoiding an unbounded in-memory `readdir()` result. Screenshot images retain the separate 8 MiB decoded-image limit.
- Focused output-cap and unrestricted file-operation regressions pass; Biome and whitespace checks pass. Exact-source run [40605155443](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent/actions/runs/40605155443) passed security and both desktop/package lifecycle jobs. Windows Node 22/24 each passed 933/955 with one known ConPTY resize failure and 21 skips; Ubuntu Node 22/24 each passed 947/955 with Bubblewrap loopback and dependent signed-MCP isolation failures and six skips. No new failure was attributed to the transport caps; this expanded edit/write ceiling change is awaiting hosted CI.

## Signed update and revocation cache recovery — 2026-10-27

- Application update, package update, and revocation refresh restore one bounded JSON backup under the feed-cache lock before sequence comparison. A missing cache primary can no longer be mistaken for a first run after an interrupted replacement, allowing a lower signed sequence to bypass rollback protection.
- Focused application-update, update-feed, and revocation-feed tests pass 27/27, including new interrupted-replacement replay regressions. Biome formatting/lint and whitespace checks pass. Exact-source hosted CI is pending.

## MCP tool result validation — 2026-10-27

- Stdio and Streamable HTTP now validate `tools/call` result envelopes, modern `resultType`, boolean `isError`, object-shaped `structuredContent`, and the standard content-block variants used by Xyst. Older negotiated protocol versions may omit `resultType`; modern versions may not. Unsupported control-flow result types remain explicitly rejected.
- The focused MCP protocol/transport suite passes 32 tests with one expected Linux-sandbox skip on Windows; the `structuredContent` shape regression is included in the current change. Biome and whitespace checks pass. Schema reference: [official MCP 2026-07-28 JSON schema](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/blob/main/schema/2026-07-28/schema.json).
- Hosted run [40603674967](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent/actions/runs/40603674967) exposed a nonconforming modern protocol response in the published example MCP package; its result now includes the required `resultType: complete`. The focused example package suite passes 3/3. Ubuntu Bubblewrap namespace failures remain a runner limitation; exact-source CI for the fix is pending.

## Control API event sequence integrity — 2026-10-27

- Control API events now assign transport sequence and timestamp after applying event data, preserving contiguous replay ordering and server-owned event times even if an emitted event contains colliding fields. The focused pause/resume API regression passes, including contiguous sequence IDs and replay after `Last-Event-ID`. Exact-source hosted CI is pending.
- Package-registry reads fail closed when only a replacement backup remains; mutations restore a single bounded, schema-valid backup under the registry lock. The focused recovery regression passes, including preserving activation state across a later disable. Exact-source hosted CI is pending.
- Runtime settings detect a missing primary with an atomic-write backup and fail closed rather than treating the settings as empty. Updates restore one bounded schema-valid backup under the settings lock before merging. Focused runtime-settings tests pass 10/10, including preserving existing preferences across recovery. Exact-source hosted CI is pending.
- Approval-rule reads now fail closed when only a replacement backup exists, and rule mutations restore a bounded schema-valid backup under the rule lock before changing policy. Focused approval-rule tests pass 9/9; two symbolic-link capability cases skip on this Windows host. Exact-source hosted CI is pending.
- Retrieval exclusion reads fail closed when only a replacement backup exists, and exclusion mutations restore one bounded validated backup under the retrieval lock. The focused recovery regression passes. Exact-source hosted CI is pending.

## Usage-ledger replacement-backup recovery — 2026-10-27

- The Windows atomic-write fallback can leave the destination absent with a UUID-named `.bak` if the process exits after moving the old file aside. The shared-lock-protected usage-ledger reader now restores exactly one bounded, regular, single-link backup only after JSON and ledger-schema validation; exclusive linking prevents overwriting a concurrently created primary. It cleans only the same-file duplicate link left by an interrupted restore and preserves ambiguous, invalid, or unsupported candidates with an explicit recovery error.
- The focused valid-recovery and ambiguous-backup tests pass (2/2) when run with local Windows hard-link capability. The default restricted local shell denies `fs.link` with `EPERM`, which the helper now reports as `ATOMIC_WRITE_RECOVERY_REQUIRED` while preserving the source backup. Formatting and exact-source hosted CI are pending. Project/global memory and goals now use the same recovery helper; subagent task history remains open.
- Project/global memory and project goals restore one validated backup under their cross-process mutation locks instead of presenting empty stores. Focused recovery regressions pass (2/2) with the required local Windows hard-link capability; exact-source hosted CI is pending.
- Subagent task-history readers now share the mutation lock, and recovery restores one bounded, schema-validated backup rather than silently reporting empty history. Ambiguous candidates are preserved and fail explicitly. Focused recovery and existing task-store/slot cases pass (3/3); exact-source hosted CI is pending.

## Session backup-recovery CI regression — 2026-10-27

- Hosted run [40599777689](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent/actions/runs/40599777689) for commit `856c4160d6fb58dbb79a246a177293a5bbe61a1b` exposed an interaction between backup recovery and ordinary missing-session handling: when neither a primary nor any backup existed, `SessionStore.load()` returned `SESSION_BACKUP_RECOVERY_REQUIRED` instead of `ENOENT`. This broke session deletion, stale-writer checks, and VS Code session lifecycle cases on both Windows and Ubuntu; other failures in the same Node matrices remain the hosted ConPTY and network-namespace limits recorded below. Security and formatting/lint passed; desktop package jobs were still in progress when inspected.
- `SessionStore.load()` now returns `ENOENT` only when there is no primary and no backup, while preserving `SESSION_BACKUP_RECOVERY_REQUIRED` for multiple or invalid recovery candidates. The focused regression plus the two hosted-failing session deletion and stale-writer cases pass locally (3/3); Biome and `git diff --check` pass. Exact-source hosted confirmation is pending.

## Windows session replacement-backup recovery — 2026-10-27

- `SessionStore.load()` now repairs the process-crash state where Windows has moved the previous session JSON to its atomic-write `.bak` but has not installed the replacement. It takes the per-session lock, accepts exactly one canonical UUID-named backup, and validates it through the existing no-follow, regular-file, single-link, size, schema, and session-ID checks before atomically restoring it.
- Missing, ambiguous, or invalid recovery backups produce `SESSION_BACKUP_RECOVERY_REQUIRED`; recovery candidates remain untouched for manual recovery. Focused tests pass for one valid backup and for preserving ambiguous backups. Biome and whitespace checks pass. Hosted verification is pending; this does not recover backups for non-session JSON stores.

## Background-process registry replacement-backup recovery — 2026-10-27

- Startup now recognizes a missing process-record primary with one UUID-named atomic-write backup. It validates the backup with the existing regular-file, single-link, size-limited, no-follow reader and record ID/status checks, then creates a no-overwrite hard link for restoration. If startup finds the same-file link left by a crash before backup-name removal, it removes only that duplicate link. Multiple or invalid backups are preserved; inability to create the hard link fails recovery explicitly without deleting the backup.
- Three focused Windows regressions pass: restoring one valid backup, cleanup of the interrupted same-file link state, and preserving ambiguous candidates. The restricted local process denied hard links (`EPERM`); the focused suite passed with the needed filesystem capability. Biome and whitespace checks pass. Hosted verification is pending.

## Hosted CI findings

- Hosted CI run `40595818168` for commit `7f6cffac7fc7fc8172c0835040af94f72709e909` verified committed-session-write recovery. The security audit and both Windows/Ubuntu desktop package, lifecycle, and native checks passed. Both Ubuntu matrices passed 926/934 tests, including the directory-flush fault injection; their two failures are the hosted Bubblewrap loopback restriction and dependent signed-MCP isolation test. Windows passed 912/934 with 21 skips and one failure, only the known ConPTY startup failure; the Linux-specific fault injection correctly skips on Windows. Formatting and lint passed on all four matrices.
- A session-store durability review found that atomic JSON writes can report a directory-sync error after the rename has already committed. `SessionStore.save` now advances the active session revision, emits a filesystem warning, and returns the committed record so the runtime does not roll back memory or conflict on retry. A Linux-only fault-injection regression simulates the post-rename flush error, reloads the committed session, then verifies the next save. Hosted Ubuntu Node 22/24 runs confirm this behavior in run `40595818168` above; Windows skips the Linux-specific boundary.
- Hosted CI run `40593790729` for `e764272f67f2d353a17dae7c265caa8c680bd44a` verified the interrupted-session recovery regression on both Node versions and operating systems. Security and both desktop package/lifecycle jobs passed. The Windows suites each passed 912/933 with 20 skips and one failure, solely the hosted ConPTY child initialization (`0xC0000142`). The Ubuntu suites each passed 925/933 with six skips and two failures, the Bubblewrap loopback setup and dependent signed-MCP isolation test (`RTM_NEWADDR: Operation not permitted`). Formatting and lint passed across all four Node matrices. Recovery code and its persisted-session regression are verified; the required OS isolation gates remain open because these hosted runners lack the necessary capabilities.
- A phase-3 crash-recovery review found that continuing a session whose saved assistant tool proposal lacked one or more results could send an invalid transcript back to the provider and leave the action outcome unclear. `AgentRuntime` now fills only missing results with explicitly untrusted “outcome unknown” observations, preserves results already persisted, and never repeats the proposed tool call during recovery. Focused recovery tests pass 2/2 and an AgentRuntime resume regression passes 1/1; Biome and `git diff --check` pass. The exact-source hosted regression is confirmed in run `40593790729` above. Crash injection across every persistence boundary remains open.
- Hosted CI run `40590698570` for commit `f16a99dbda9f5bd150d7edc55f134053c01fca0a` passed the security audit and both desktop packaging jobs, including Windows NSIS install/reinstall/uninstall, packaged AppContainer helper and native desktop checks, Ubuntu DEB install/upgrade/rollback, AppImage Xvfb smoke, and native desktop checks. The Linux Node 22 run completed 922/930 tests with six expected platform skips; its two failures were the required signed-MCP and Bubblewrap integration checks blocked by hosted loopback namespace setup (`RTM_NEWADDR: Operation not permitted`). Both Linux versions report the same runner limitation. Windows Node 22/24 each fail only the required ConPTY integration at child initialization (`0xC0000142`). The MCP control-flow regressions were included in the full run and passed. All currently fixable gates for this revision pass; required platform isolation gates remain open.

## Automatic search and current desktop packages — 2026-10-27

- **Build qualification baseline:** initial source snapshot `f735cea7f555ebbbd1b41af4596c089a98b700cf` is preserved in history. The current commit-backed build work is published to `iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent` on `codex/xyst-ai-assistant-v0.1.2`; existing `main` and `codex/resume-full-build` branches are preserved. The reproducible development source archive includes `BUILD_PLAN.md`, and all four development artifact hashes are recorded in `dist/dev/SHA256SUMS.txt`. The current Windows NSIS installer SHA-256 is `e7bd3e7def2acbf577e7f872b608b0235dfb2b3decd32063c588f8eaf489c574`; it is `NotSigned`. Hosted CI, independent security review, and native Ubuntu Desktop/Wayland acceptance remain open, so this is a development build, not a release-qualified build.
- A local lint/format pass found a leftover `goal_order` validation from an abandoned goal-reordering attempt. Removed the unsupported check and its ignored project-state field; Biome lint and format now pass across 238 files. No runtime behavior changed and no test suite was run.

- The saved configuration uses Automatic search with no search key; the LM Studio profiles have no model credential references. In the normal Windows user environment, GitHub, Wikipedia, and LM Studio's `127.0.0.1:1234/v1/models` endpoint each returned HTTP 200. A live search from the installed Xyst runtime sent four requests with zero credential headers and returned five Google News RSS/Wikipedia results; GDELT was unavailable and fallback succeeded. Earlier `ENOTFOUND` and local server failures occurred in the restricted execution shell and did not describe the normal host network. If all public sources really fail, Xyst's new error explicitly says Automatic search needs no API key and directs the user to check network/source availability.
- The Windows NSIS installer with current-user manual-proxy support was rebuilt from source. Focused Windows Rust proxy tests passed 2/2. Its isolated install/runtime/reinstall/uninstall smoke passed, including service/API/UI readiness, all 11 GUI modules, 120-DPI resize and workspace restoration, configuration retention, and restoration of the prior uninstall registration. Current installer SHA-256: `e7bd3e7def2acbf577e7f872b608b0235dfb2b3decd32063c588f8eaf489c574`; Authenticode status is `NotSigned`. The first sandboxed build could not download NSIS; the same pinned build completed after network access was allowed. The sandboxed smoke could not read uninstall registration; the isolated smoke completed with registry access and restored the prior entry.
- The Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 DEB (SHA-256 `d1d73609b93584a022935fdf41f3e6feafd33959e74d4506b4b169130f5194f8`) passed its source, install/upgrade/rollback/purge, service/UI, settings retention, resize, and accessibility smoke. The AppImage (SHA-256 `ea4af2214ac68084dcf764180528e72046a43ac04e38143bf9ca37dceec254ff`) passed source, service/UI, and Xvfb desktop/accessibility smoke. Windows and Linux agent/search suites passed 129/129; the focused search suite passed 15/15 twice on each OS. Biome lint/format, whitespace checks, and two 3/3 release-archive test runs pass. The deterministic source archive and four-artifact development checksum manifest are refreshed after this status update.
- Production gates remain open: native Ubuntu Desktop/Wayland acceptance, hosted CI, ARM64, full desktop/IDE and contract coverage, independent security review, and signing/update governance.

# Keyless news fallback and live-source verification — 2026-10-27

- Rebuilt all three development installers/packages after the TOML parser fix. The Windows NSIS installer passed isolated install, service/API/UI and 11-module checks, 120-DPI resize, last-workspace restoration, in-place reinstall with configuration retention, uninstall, and registry restoration. The Ubuntu DEB passed source integrity, install, upgrade to 0.1.3, rollback to 0.1.2, purge, settings retention, service/UI checks, resize, accessibility, and automation; the AppImage passed source integrity and full Xvfb desktop/service smoke. SHA-256: Windows NSIS `8bfd9b737420dfd3a4ad074c744556b48bd526a01b245ebb3d4ac76b8d3e841a`; Linux DEB `8825e6f9c6ea08ec1338a9f1b1e3c171a49abd766da024966fdb6ffa543fab64`; Linux AppImage `8c5e7adda528244999cb13812d3b079e8b538fa921c04e7392fe1b1d363075ae`. Their hashes are recorded in `dist/dev/SHA256SUMS.txt`; the source archive and manifest were regenerated after this status update.
- The TOML parser now rejects illegal later extensions to inline tables while allowing valid nested sibling dotted keys. Full Windows `pnpm check` passes twice (894/922, zero failures, 28 platform/capability skips); full Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 `pnpm check:linux` passes twice (916/922, zero failures, six Windows-only skips). Both runs syntax-check all 236 JavaScript modules; Linux CI-required Bubblewrap, pidfd, reparse-point, and Secret Service integrations ran. The initial Linux attempt hit a full host C: drive and WSL `EIO`; after removing the disposable 1.46 GB Cargo target under `.test-tmp`, ext4 write/fsync recovered and two full runs passed. Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass. A real Automatic lookup without credentials returned Google News RSS and Wikipedia results while GDELT was unavailable, exercising the intended fallback. The development source archive and checksum manifest were regenerated from this source and verified.
- Re-ran the packaged Windows AppContainer helper integration: it passes the shared process path, reads and edits a pre-existing nested workspace file, checks workspace writes and read-only denial, verifies private temp handling, blocks sibling writes, and exercises failure-closed process rights. The earlier file-based output-fixture attempt returned `Access is denied` in this constrained host; the passing nested-file helper integration shows that result does not establish a product workspace-ACL failure. The Windows ConPTY TUI resize test could not run here: its child process exits with NTSTATUS `0xC0000142` even under elevated tool execution, consistent with this low-integrity sandbox. Windows CI sets `XYST_REQUIRE_WINDOWS_CONPTY_TEST=true`; native CI evidence remains required.
- The Windows desktop AppContainer stdout/stderr regression now generates both large payloads from bounded shell environment values instead of a slow 110,000-iteration loop. The previous fixture hit its 20-second timeout on this host; a file-based replacement was denied because the file existed before AppContainer applied inherited workspace permissions. The corrected test fills both pipes concurrently above the 1 MiB capture cap and completes in under one second. Two full Windows desktop Rust runs each pass (36 passed, zero failed, two expected symbolic-link-privilege skips). The serialized integration-test lock now recovers after a panic so one fixture failure cannot cascade into unrelated tests. Rust formatting and strict Clippy pass. The environment is a low-integrity desktop sandbox; elevated execution was needed for Windows ACL/reparse fixtures. Hosted CI and native Windows client gates remain authoritative for release qualification.
- Automatic lookup now queries GDELT, Google News RSS, and Wikipedia concurrently. Google News RSS supplies current article titles, links, sources, and publication times when GDELT is slow or unavailable; no search API key is used. Bounded RSS parsing rejects unsafe links, caps responses/results, and applies request timeouts and rate limits. A live Windows Automatic lookup returned five results across Google News RSS and Wikipedia while identifying GDELT as unavailable; live Open-Meteo current weather also responded without credentials. GDELT timed out from this network. The focused web-search suite passed 14/14 twice, lint passed, and full Windows `pnpm check` passed twice (893/921, zero failures, 28 expected platform/capability skips) before the newest agent regression; all 236 JavaScript modules were syntax-checked. The final agent/provider suites pass 128/128 twice on Windows and Linux.

# MCP HTTP response-boundary hardening — 2026-10-26

- Fixed malformed UTF-8 JSON responses being misreported as transport failures; they now return the protocol-level `MCP_INVALID_RESPONSE` code. `Content-Length` values above the response limit are rejected and cancelled before reading; streamed bodies without a trustworthy length remain capped during reads.
- Server discovery now validates protocol-version lists as arrays of at most 32 date-form strings. It rejects malformed values (including strings that merely contain the supported version) before protocol selection.
- Added regressions for malformed UTF-8, early cancellation of oversized declared bodies, streamed over-limit bodies without `Content-Length`, and malformed server discovery version lists.
- The updated MCP suite passed twice at 27/28, zero failures, with one expected Linux-only sandbox skip. Full Windows suite: 866/891 passed, zero failed, 25 expected platform/capability skips. Full Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 suite: 885/891 passed, zero failed, six expected Windows-only skips. Biome lint/format, all 230 JavaScript syntax checks, and `git diff --check` passed. The Linux and Windows package smokes compare `src/tools/mcp-http.mjs` with the packaged source.
- Rebuilt the current 87.10 MiB Linux DEB from strict discovery-version validation source. Its source-integrity/service smoke passed twice, followed by install, upgrade/rollback, config retention, Xvfb desktop/accessibility/automation, and purge smoke. Artifact: `dist/releases/linux/Xyst AI Assistant_0.1.2_amd64.deb`; SHA-256 `d3eb3a61b59f5406058ed8137b517c15f94b618dbfa39be857bc330a39f91dd2`.
- Rebuilt the 27.80 MiB Windows x64 NSIS installer from the same source. The smoke verified `src/tools/mcp-http.mjs` byte-for-byte and passed isolated install, service startup/shutdown, 120-DPI resize, workspace restoration, reinstall/config retention, uninstall, and registry restoration. Artifact: `dist/releases/Xyst AI Assistant_0.1.2_x64-setup.exe`; SHA-256 `6b75df6e73cf3b8e0bfa92dc4adcb833f66fed66ddef3115dceb469b64ba9a9a`.

# Watch-backed semantic content cache — 2026-10-25

- Added an optional recursive workspace watcher to `SemanticWorkspaceIndex`. The long-lived authenticated API semantic index enables it and closes it with the server; one-shot index/search calls and per-run `AgentRuntime` indexes remain watcher-free. When active, each search still verifies file identity and stat metadata, reuses unchanged in-memory text and hashes, invalidates changed/renamed paths from notifications, and fully rereads content at least every 30 seconds. Unknown filenames, watcher errors, incomplete scans, and unsupported watcher creation clear cached content and fall back to full reads. A change during scan triggers an uncached retry; repeated changes mark the result truncated.
- Regressions cover cache hits, watcher invalidation, unknown-filename invalidation, the 30-second refresh, watcher-error fallback, and a real 512-file nested save burst that confirms recursive filesystem notifications invalidate cached paths and refresh results. Every changed file is re-embedded. The semantic-index suite passes 13/13 on Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 and 12 with one expected Windows symlink-privilege skip. The combined retrieval and semantic-index suites pass 19/19 on WSL; Windows passes 17 with two expected link-privilege skips. Agent semantic paths pass 3/3 and the authenticated semantic API test passes 1/1.
- At the 2,400-file × 2-KiB/4,800-chunk benchmark scale, watcher-active warm search is 2,363.19 ms on Windows x64/Node 24.22.1 and 690.32 ms on Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 x64/Node 24.19.0, compared with the prior uncached 4,197.27 ms and 2,574.37 ms respectively. Both report 2,400 cached documents. These are host-specific measurements with deterministic in-process embeddings. Large save-burst/overflow soak and notification-loss testing remain open.

# Bounded retrieval policy-file reads — 2026-10-25

- Added a shared no-follow bounded reader for workspace retrieval policy files. `.gitignore` is capped at 1 MiB and 10,000 lines, while semantic `excludes.json` is capped at 32 KiB. Opened-handle metadata and the current path are rechecked after reading so size/path replacement races fail closed. Both line search and semantic indexing use it, including the exclusions status API.
- Regressions cover oversized and overlong `.gitignore` files, oversized `excludes.json`, and replacement of `.gitignore` between metadata validation and open. The retrieval and semantic-index suites pass 17/17 on Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4, including symlink/junction swap tests. Windows passes 15 with one expected link-privilege skip. Biome, syntax, and whitespace checks pass.

# Maximum-index retrieval soak — 2026-10-25

- Ran the corrected deterministic benchmark at 2,400 files × 2 KiB, yielding 4,800 chunks, below the 5,000-chunk index cap. It completed without truncation on Windows x64/Node 24.22.1: initial index/search 6,245.48 ms, warm search 4,197.27 ms, 1,413,780-byte index, RSS delta +97.70 MiB. Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 x64/Node 24.19.0: initial 3,201.19 ms, warm search 2,574.37 ms, same index size, RSS delta +94.95 MiB. Both issued 150 chunk batches capped at 32 inputs and two query embeddings.
- The soak verifies the current chunk ceiling on both platforms. Warm searches still rescan source contents, so lower-cost freshness tracking remains an open performance task. Measurements use a deterministic in-process embedding callback and are host-specific.

# Retrieval resource ceiling validation — 2026-10-25

- Added hard limits to direct workspace-search options and config validation: at most 100 results, 10,000 candidate files, and 1,000,000 bytes per file. This aligns keyword and semantic retrieval bounds; configured values can no longer bypass line-search result or file-count limits. Added direct-call and configuration regressions for each upper bound.
- Retrieval and configuration suites pass 28/28 on Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4, including the directory-swap race test. Windows passes 27 with one expected symlink-privilege skip. Biome, JavaScript syntax, and whitespace checks pass.

# Line-search file-count cap enforcement — 2026-10-25

- Line-level workspace search checked `maxFiles` only in its outer directory loop, so it could continue scanning additional regular files in the current directory. The file loop now stops immediately at the cap and sets `truncated` when entries or queued directories remain. Added a deterministic regression confirming one file is scanned, the first result is returned, and the partial scan is reported.
- `node --test tests/retrieval.test.mjs` passes (3 passed, one expected Windows symlink-privilege skip); syntax, Biome, and whitespace checks pass. The reparse-point race test was not exercised in this sandboxed run because Windows link creation returned `EPERM`.

# Hybrid retrieval benchmark fixture correction — 2026-10-25

- The scale benchmark now uses one exact phrase in one synthetic document. This exercises standalone line-match fusion while avoiding a fixture that repeats the query enough times to trigger the line search's intentional 25-result truncation. The benchmark asserts a complete scan and that the exact phrase reaches results.
- A 1,500-file × 2-KiB run indexed 1,500 files and 3,000 chunks without truncation on Windows x64/Node 24.22.1: generation 4,056.82 ms, initial index/search 3,406.99 ms, warm search 2,227.17 ms, index 883,680 bytes, RSS delta +72.25 MiB. Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 x64/Node 24.19.0: generation 392.63 ms, initial 1,387.68 ms, warm 1,235.84 ms, same index size, RSS delta +73.21 MiB. Each run issued 94 chunk-embedding batches (maximum 32 inputs) and two query embeddings. Timings are machine-specific and use a deterministic in-process embedding callback.
- The first benchmark attempt intentionally failed its new no-truncation assertion because the original fixture repeated the exact phrase on many lines; the fixture was corrected and both platform runs then completed. Watcher behavior, large-repository soak, real-provider latency, and the residual parent-directory race review remain open.

# Atomic native startup-state persistence — 2026-10-25

- Last-workspace and window-bounds state now share a write-through atomic writer. It creates exclusive temp files, flushes contents, atomically replaces the destination on Windows via `MoveFileExW` with `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH`, and flushes the parent directory on Unix. Unix state files use mode `0600`. Startup restoration opens the state without following links (Unix `O_NOFOLLOW`, Windows `FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT`), rejects symlink/reparse-point and hard-linked state, validates opened metadata, and caps reads at 32 KiB. Regressions cover permissions, hard links, Linux symlinks, malformed/oversized/stale state. Windows desktop-host tests pass (35 passed, two expected symlink-privilege skips); Ubuntu WSL tests pass 10/10. `pnpm check:desktop` and strict Clippy pass on Windows and Linux. The current-source Ubuntu WSL X11 desktop smoke now confirms resize from 1440×920 to 760×540, persisted state, and size restoration after restart. Native Windows resize/restart and physical Ubuntu Desktop/Wayland validation remain open.

# Durable verified application-update staging — 2026-10-25

- The verified installer download is now written through one exclusive, private `FileHandle`; partial writes are retried until each chunk is complete, data is flushed before success, and the same open handle is compared with the staged path's device/inode/size/link count to detect replacement during verification. Regressions force short writes, assert the flush happens before return, swap the staged path before identity comparison, and abort after the first network chunk to verify rejection and cleanup. The application-update suite passes 12/12 on Windows and 12/12 on Ubuntu 24.04 WSL. The latest full suites pass on Windows (843 tests: 819 passed, 24 expected platform/capability skips, zero failures) and Ubuntu WSL (843 tests: 838 passed, five Windows-only skips, zero failures); both syntax-check 224 modules. Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass. Full package update handoff/install/rollback validation remains an open release gate.

# Atomic file-write durability — 2026-10-25

- Shared atomic writes now flush temporary file contents before publishing them. Linux commits also flush the containing directory after rename so directory metadata is pushed with the file data; Windows flushes file contents and relies on its replacement semantics because directory flushing through Node is not portable there. If a Linux directory flush fails after rename, the error carries `committed: true`, and regression verifies the new content is present. Windows focused file/tool checks pass (21 passed, four expected platform/capability skips); Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 focused checks pass (25/25, including file flush ordering, post-rename directory flush, and committed-state reporting). Lint, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. Full Windows and Linux suites for this specific durability change remain pending.

# Strict provider `Retry-After` parsing — 2026-10-25

- `Retry-After` now accepts integer delta-seconds or a canonical HTTP date, caps server delays at 60 seconds before the runtime's shorter 5-second backoff cap, clamps past dates to zero, and ignores fractional/invalid values rather than coercing them through permissive date parsing. Provider tests cover seconds, cap, future/past dates, malformed dates, and invalid numeric values.
- Provider suite passes 45/45. Two Windows full checks pass (838 tests discovered, 816 passed, 22 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 224 modules syntax-checked). Two Ubuntu WSL ext4 checks pass (838 discovered, 833 passed, five Windows-only skips, zero failures; 224 modules syntax-checked).

# Windows atomic replacement fallback tests — 2026-10-25

- Added cross-platform simulated-Windows regressions for the backup-and-replace path: successful replacement removes the backup; failed commit restores the original; and failed rollback preserves the original backup and attaches the restore error to the commit error. This covers handled failures; recovery after abrupt process termination remains open.
- `tests/platform-files.test.mjs` passes 3/3. Two full Windows checks pass (838 tests discovered, 816 passed, 22 expected platform/capability skips, zero failures; 224 modules syntax-checked). Two Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 checks pass (838 discovered, 833 passed, five Windows-only skips, zero failures; 224 modules syntax-checked).

# Bounded edit-file reads — 2026-10-25

- `edit_file` now reads from its already checked file handle in bounded stream chunks. It stops when the configured byte cap is crossed instead of using `readFile()` after only an initial size check. The regression grows a small file to 4 MiB after the size check, verifies the edit is rejected, and confirms the concurrent change remains intact.
- The focused file-tool suite passes 19/19 on Ubuntu WSL. Two final Windows full checks pass (835 tests discovered, 813 passed, 22 expected platform/capability skips, zero failures; 223 modules syntax-checked). Two Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 checks pass (835 discovered, 830 passed, five Windows-only skips, zero failures; 223 modules syntax-checked). Lint, formatting, and whitespace checks pass.

# Atomic file replacement permission preservation — 2026-10-25

- Atomic file replacement now preserves existing regular files' POSIX permission bits, including execute permissions; newly created files retain the private `0600` default. Added a file-tool regression that edits and overwrites an executable script and verifies its mode after each operation. Windows mode-bit coverage is skipped because Windows permissions are ACL-based.
- The file-tool suite passes 18/18 on Ubuntu WSL, with the mode regression executed. Two final Windows checks pass (834 tests, 812 passed, 22 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 223 JavaScript modules checked each run). Two final Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 checks pass (834 tests, 829 passed, five Windows-only skips, zero failures; 223 modules checked each run). Lint, formatting, and whitespace checks pass.

# Same-profile provider retries and backoff — 2026-10-25

- Model profiles now support 0–2 automatic same-profile retries (default 1) for retryable rate-limit/server responses and known pre-response connection failures. Backoff is bounded, honors a capped `Retry-After`, and stops promptly on cancellation. Timed-out requests remain fallback-only to avoid replaying an operation the provider may have accepted. Retry exhaustion charges uncertain input conservatively and releases failed output even without a fallback chain. Runtime Settings exposes the retry count; retry lifecycle events are typed and included in the observability allowlist.
- Agent/provider/config/runtime-override focused coverage passed, including a retry-exhaustion ledger regression. Two final Windows full checks pass (833 tests discovered, 812 passed, 21 expected platform/capability skips, zero failures; 223 modules syntax-checked). Two Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 checks pass (833 discovered, 828 passed, five Windows-only skips, zero failures; 223 modules syntax-checked). Lint, formatting, and whitespace checks pass.
- Offline agent evaluations pass 14/14 and routing evaluations pass 22/22 after the retry changes.

# Review-mode structured output and evidence repair — 2026-10-25

- Review's final provider turn now requests a strict JSON Schema object and includes parsed `read_file` content in a compact evidence block labeled untrusted, preserving the source tool-call ID and line range. The final turn omits tool-protocol messages after inspection so models receive direct source/test text. Malformed early Review output retries once as a tool-free structured assessment; a bounded compatibility retry handles providers that reject JSON Schema.
- Repeated live acceptance against the loaded `qwen3-coder-30b-a3b-instruct` at LM Studio's reported 8,192-token context reads both fixture files and reports the known contract mismatch at `src/retry-policy.mjs:3` with medium severity and source evidence. The fixture explicitly states the strict retry cap and bounded impact; one fixture cannot establish general review quality for all models.
- Focused agent/provider/review suites pass twice (142/142 each). Two full Windows checks pass: 823 discovered, 802 passed, 21 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 223 JavaScript modules syntax-checked. `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, and `git diff --check` pass.

# Provider transient HTTP classification — 2026-10-25

- Resolved a mismatch where HTTP 500 errors carried `retryable: true` but `HTTP_ERROR`, so the configured model fallback logic did not recognize them. HTTP 500/502/503/504 now map to retryable `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`; 501/505 remain non-retryable `HTTP_ERROR`. Added table-driven provider tests and AgentRuntime fallback coverage for HTTP 500. This is configured cross-profile failover before streamed text, not same-profile request replay.
- `docs/CONFIGURATION.md` now matches the accepted statuses. Focused provider and agent tests passed twice (140/140 each). Two consecutive full Windows `pnpm check` runs passed: 824 tests discovered, 803 passed, 21 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 223 JavaScript modules syntax-checked each run. `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, `git diff --check`, and `pnpm eval:agent` (14/14 scenarios) pass. The 21 skips are host/capability-specific and listed by `pnpm check`; Linux-only behaviors still require Linux CI.

# Request-timeout fallback and conservative accounting — 2026-10-25

- Request timeouts now use the configured fallback chain when no streamed text has been emitted. Timeout and transient server failures are treated as uncertain provider acceptance, so bounded input usage and configured input cost are settled conservatively while output reservation is released. Added an AgentRuntime timeout-failover regression covering the ledger. Focused provider and agent suites pass 141/141. Two consecutive full Windows `pnpm check` runs pass (825 tests discovered, 804 passed, 21 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 223 JavaScript modules syntax-checked each run). `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, `git diff --check`, and `pnpm eval:agent` (14/14 scenarios) pass. Two consecutive Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 `pnpm check:linux` runs pass (820 passed, five Windows-only skips, zero failures; 223 JavaScript modules syntax-checked each run).

- Chat responses now reject malformed assistant content and tool-call object/function fields. Streaming rejects invalid choices/deltas, content types, tool-call lists/fragments, indices, function fragments, and finish-reason types before accepting a terminal marker. Added regressions for shapes that previously could yield a successful empty response. The focused provider suite passes 44/44 twice, including chunked-body limit cancellation and malformed UTF-8 rejection; the combined provider/agent suite passes 145/145. Two Windows full runs immediately before these provider-only test additions passed 827 tests (806 passed, 21 platform/capability skips, zero failures); two Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 full runs passed 822/827 (five Windows-only skips, zero failures). - The coding and delegation smoke previously used the model catalog's advertised context, which LM Studio reported as larger than the loaded instance's configured context. The live preflight now reads the bounded, read-only `/api/v1/models` status, confirms the selected LM Studio model is loaded, and uses its loaded `config.context_length`; generic OpenAI-compatible local servers retain the discovered-context fallback. The pause/resume smoke shares this preflight and uses the same effective context.

Two Windows `pnpm check` runs pass 797/818 each (21 expected platform/capability skips, zero failures; 222 modules checked), and two Ubuntu 24.04 WSL `pnpm check:linux` runs pass 813/818 each (five Windows-only skips, zero failures; 222 modules checked). LM Studio currently has no loaded vision-capable model, so live image-context behavior remains open.
- Live provider usage exposed two recovery defects: LM Studio reports context overflow as `exceed_context_size_error` with “exceeds the available context size,” which was misclassified as `BAD_REQUEST`; after classification, recovery also depended on the local estimate exceeding a threshold despite a provider rejection. The provider adapter now maps this LM Studio response to `CONTEXT_OVERFLOW`. Explicit overflow recovery drops older complete active-turn exchanges and replaces the latest oversized tool result with a small omission marker while preserving its assistant tool call, paired tool response, original request, and full saved transcript. Recovery instructions tell the model to reread only bounded ranges. Regressions cover the provider's nested error message, estimate-independent trimming, retention of the latest pairing, and a single oversized result.
- The live LM Studio overflow smoke passes on `qwen3-coder-30b-a3b-instruct` with the actual 8,192-token loaded context. A calibrated run triggered LM Studio's real context rejection at 8,818 tokens, and Xyst recovered by compacting one large result. A batched-tool run then exposed a second case: two large results in one tool exchange need compaction together. The context projection now compacts every tool result in the current batch while preserving pairings and the full transcript. The final live run rejected an 8,993-token request, compacted both results, and returned exactly `OVERFLOW_RECOVERED` in three model calls with no refused approvals. The fixture planner supports loaded contexts from 4,096 to 65,536 tokens while keeping synthetic files within tool limits. Focused provider/agent/context/live-smoke tests pass 154/154; the live-smoke harness passes 11/11; lint, format, and whitespace checks pass. Two authorized Windows full checks pass at 797/818 each (21 expected skips), and two Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 checks pass at 813/818 each (five Windows-only skips); both platforms syntax-check 222 JavaScript modules.

# Persistent Ink terminal UI follow-up — 2026-10-25

- Capped terminal-rendered conversation messages and streamed drafts at 12,000 characters and approval previews at 50,000 characters. Text is bounded before secret redaction to prevent slow near-quadratic scans on oversized plain text; clipped private-key blocks are closed before redaction so their displayed prefix is not exposed. Approval preview paging now uses the same non-empty line count as rendering.
- Added regressions for clipped private-key redaction and an end-to-end Linux pseudo-terminal run with a mock streaming provider. The UI streams the answer, presents a write preview, denies the requested edit without creating the file, returns to the prompt, and exits on `/exit`. A second live stream is cancelled with Ctrl+C; the provider socket closes and the saved session retains partial output with `cancelled` status.
- The prompt accepts Ctrl+J and terminal-reported Shift+Enter as line breaks while preserving regular Enter for submission; pasted CRLF text is normalized to `\n`. Cursor-based editing uses Unicode grapheme boundaries for Left/Right, Up/Down, Home/End, Backspace, and Delete; Up/Down keeps its preferred grapheme column across short lines, and on single-line prompts it browses history. Ctrl+U/Ctrl+K edit the current line. Prompt rendering marks the cursor position, and pasted ANSI/control sequences are discarded. The Linux PTY regression edits both lines of a prompt and verifies the exact provider input.

# Failed agent sessions retain and show diagnostics — 2026-10-24

- The saved-session investigation found two prior Qwen3 Coder runs with zero model tokens and one interrupted Review session with four successful read-only tools but no final response. Those records do not include provider error details, so their exact causes cannot be reconstructed. - Context-overflow errors now trigger one context-only recovery: the oldest complete tool exchanges from the active request are omitted from the provider projection, the latest exchange and original user request remain, and the full saved transcript is unchanged. The system message tells the model to reread before relying on omitted observations and not to repeat edits blindly. The UI reports when earlier tool calls were omitted. If the reduced request still cannot fit, the run stops with `context_overflow`. Added tests for LM Studio error classification, paired tool-history trimming, no transcript mutation, the agent retry, and failure persistence/import. The live model smoke that exposed the issue failed before this repair; a post-fix live rerun remains necessary.
- Session import previously forced every imported session to `active`, even when it carried a persisted `failed` status and failure details. Imports now retain `failed`; a new run clears the old failure metadata as before. Reopening a failed session renders its sanitized error activity. Browser coverage checks reopening the record. The browser smoke passed after one unrelated transient local-model-card assertion. Focused tests and cross-platform full checks are tracked in the current execution map.
- A separate routing heuristic prefers an explicitly coder-specialized model ID when quality scores are missing or tied for tool-using coding tasks. That is only a tie-break hint, not a quality benchmark. The coder model must be configured in Xyst before routing can choose it. A pinned model still takes precedence.

## Paused-run autonomy and sandbox changes — 2026-10-24

- Runtime Settings can now update autonomy or sandbox policy while all active API runs are paused. The API persists the setting, queues a snapshot for each paused run, and rejects policy changes while any run is executing or starting. Resume applies the policy at the next safe checkpoint, refreshes the available tools and budget rules, and switches system policy; moving from Unrestricted to Assisted redacts the existing transcript before it is sent again. The GUI reports when the change is queued and active.
- Regression coverage verifies that a running run rejects the change, a paused run applies an Unrestricted setting on resume so its next write no longer asks for approval, and a sandbox mode update refreshes the tools offered to the model. Focused API and agent regressions pass; `pnpm lint` and `pnpm format:check` pass.
- Two full Windows `pnpm check` runs pass 766/786 each (20 expected platform/capability skips, zero failures; 217 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Ubuntu WSL verification and release package rebuilds are still pending for this change.
- Live LM Studio Qwen results remain mixed: previous file and command operations succeeded, but both repeated runs missed the exact `VERIFIED` final response. Reliable model selection and exact final-output compliance remain open.

## Agent run shutdown persistence regression — 2026-10-24

- The newest saved Xyst review session in this workspace was still marked `active` after four successful read-only tool results and had no final assistant response. Repeating the same review against a disposable copy of its two assigned files completed in two model calls, so the stored transcript was interrupted before the final response; the exact interruption cause is not recorded in the session.
- `LocalControlServer` previously launched run execution without retaining its promise. Shutdown aborted runs and closed the HTTP listener but could return before cancellation reached `AgentRuntime` and persisted the session's terminal status. Shutdown now closes the listener, aborts current runs, waits for accepted requests to settle, then drains all run promises before closing shared services. A regression delays provider cancellation and verifies shutdown returns only after the run and persisted session are marked `cancelled`.
- The focused shutdown and paused-run cancellation tests pass 2/2. Two full Windows `pnpm check` runs pass 764/784 each (20 expected platform/capability skips, zero failures; 217 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Ubuntu 24.04 WSL `pnpm check:linux` passes 775/780 (five Windows-only skips, zero failures; 216 modules syntax-checked). Biome lint and format checks pass.
- Two current live `qwen3-coder-30b-a3b-instruct` runs both read the two fixture sources, wrote and read back both expected files, and passed the process and shell workflows. Both runs still failed the exact `VERIFIED` final-response check. This confirms the tool and file workflow while showing that exact final-output compliance remains inconsistent; it is not a general model-quality measurement.

## LM Studio capability discovery and live agent check — 2026-10-24

- Live Qwen 3 4B smoke confirmed the configured model can read both source files, write the two expected results, read them back, and run the exact approved process and shell commands. The model initially failed the exact `VERIFIED` final-response check despite correct work; the agent policy and regression test now explicitly require honoring exact user-requested final text after verifying outcomes. The rerun passed both file and command workflows and returned the exact markers. The model was unloaded after the run.
- LM Studio's loopback model catalog exposes explicit `trained_for_tool_use` and `vision` flags. Local discovery now passes these booleans through the authenticated API, new model profiles use them instead of assuming every model is tool-trained, and the Settings model list displays reported tool/vision capabilities. Missing metadata keeps the existing compatibility default; Xyst does not infer coding quality from model names or parameter counts.

Browser smoke asserts capability labels and the saved local profile and passes. A live discovery call reports the installed Qwen 3 4B profile as tool-trained, text-only, and not loaded. Current complete cross-platform checks are recorded in the provider/package verification section above. Native Ubuntu Desktop and hosted CI remain separate release gates.

## Coding-agent routing and LM Studio model loading — 2026-10-24

- Fixed the coding-run path so implementation requests use the recent user context to require a tool-capable profile. Plan and Review continue to require tools; ordinary programming questions may use text-only models. A manually pinned model remains explicit, but a text-only pin now fails before the provider call when the task needs implementation tools. The agent instructions now emphasize preserving complete files, avoiding placeholder rewrites, verifying changes, and reporting the actual result rather than treating a plan as implementation.

The picker retains Auto across automatic runs while manual profile selection remains a per-run pin.

## Agent delegation and unborn-repository worktrees — 2026-10-24

- Delegation is available through the Runtime Settings child-agent toggle and concurrency limit. The agent prompt now asks it to delegate independent work when the tool is enabled, inspect each child's changed paths, verify integrated work, and never claim an unused delegation. Automatic model routing still does not override an explicit model pin.
- Child worktrees now support repositories with no `HEAD`: Xyst builds a private baseline commit from Git-visible project files using a temporary index, excludes `.agent` state, and leaves the user's working tree, index, and `HEAD` untouched. Git filters are disabled for each snapshot operation, including `write-tree`, and for child-change inspection and worktree cleanup. Git repository/index override variables are removed from the safe child-process environment.

## Windows process-creation race recovery follow-up — 2026-10-23

- Expanded the recovered Windows process regression to trigger a bounded second wave of nested processes as the restarted manager attaches the recorded process tree to a kill-on-close Job Object. The test observes new process IDs during recovery, verifies the recorded descendants are gone, and checks that no nested heartbeat writer continues after termination. This proves ordinary nested `CreateProcess` descendants created during attachment inherit or are captured by the job; it does not cover WMI-created processes whose OS parent is outside the recovered tree.
- The focused Windows race test passes twice. Two consecutive full Windows `pnpm check` runs pass 733 tests each (713 passed, 20 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 205 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Two consecutive synchronized Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 `pnpm check:linux` runs pass 733 each (729 passed, four Windows-only skips, zero failures; required Linux integrations ran, 205 modules syntax-checked). The latest Linux run confirms Windows-only skips have explicit reasons. `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, and whitespace checks pass.
- WMI/out-of-tree process attribution, physical Ubuntu Desktop recovery, hosted CI, broader sandbox crash boundaries, and independent security review remain open.

## Default prompt-aware model routing — 2026-10-23

- Enabled the bounded local heuristic by default for unpinned agent runs using balanced routing; `[routing.classifier] enabled = false` restores the configured balanced choice. It samples at most 12,000 characters from the beginning/end of oversized prompts, never sends content to a separate model, and leaves ambiguous requests on the configured balanced route. Clear brevity requests use configured speed scores; substantial implementation/testing/security prompts use coding quality scores. Manual profile pins, review/plan roles, configured non-balanced strategies, and strict fallback chains retain precedence. Sessions record only intent and signal counts. Automatic choices use configured model metadata rather than guessing quality from model IDs.
- Expanded the offline routing set to 20 curated synthetic cases. The nine applicable classifier cases show seven improved selections, zero regressions, and two unchanged cases (100% versus 22.2% deterministic baseline). This does not measure real model answer quality, latency, or request cost; the local heuristic remains reversible and its profile metadata and real-task performance need continued review.
- Focused provider/routing tests pass 34/34 after the bounded sampling adjustment. The full focused provider/config/agent/routing set also passes under Ubuntu 24.04 WSL (139/139) using the current Windows-mounted source tree. See the cross-platform verification section above for current full-suite and quality-check results. Hosted CI verification remains a separate release gate.

## Explicit subagent retry orchestration — 2026-10-23

- Added `retry_subagent_task` to the parent agent. It retries only stopped, failed, cancelled, or timed-out attempts from the same parent session, uses a fresh child session and Git worktree, records `attempt`/`retry_of` lineage, preserves earlier worktrees, and refuses overlapping retries. When a child throws before returning a run result, the parent receives its task ID in the error so it can still retry or inspect the failure. A force-kill recovery test verifies a recovered attempt can be retried after its owner process is proven absent.
- Documented the parent tool and attempt fields in the configuration/API guides and updated the recovery roadmap. Focused agent, subagent, and tool suites pass 112/112. Two full Windows checks pass (728 total: 709 passed, 19 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 205 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Ubuntu `pnpm check:linux` passes (728 total: 724 passed, four Windows-only skips, zero failures; 205 modules syntax-checked). Lint, formatting, VS Code package validation, and whitespace checks pass.
- Rebuilt and smoke-tested the Windows NSIS installer (25.60 MiB; SHA-256 `1D0835084138C838EE2B8F0F8A30178D19EB30CF31382DD9B3B5EB207DDBF072`) and Ubuntu DEB (49.97 MiB; SHA-256 `2BEEC36E17EDB32318586B2ED1973941BA8EFA3F2605D3EA698962A908BA2FC9`). Windows install/reinstall/uninstall and Linux install/purge, service/UI, accessibility, and configuration-retention smokes pass. The 332-file source archive reproduces byte-for-byte on Windows and Ubuntu.
- Reviewed the existing security threat model against the retry and crash-recovery behavior, documented the verified recovery→retry boundary and remaining cross-platform cleanup risks, and closed the roadmap's threat-model documentation item. Native containment review remains an independent release gate.
- Retry orchestration is now implemented; broader crash-boundary coverage, Windows descendant recovery, and background temporary-file cleanup remain open.

## Child-only orchestration cancellation — 2026-10-23

- Added authenticated `POST /v1/subagents/{task_id}/cancel` and the matching control-client method. The API and every parent-run `AgentRuntime` share one task store, which keeps live cancellation callbacks in memory and aborts only the selected child signal. It refuses finished tasks and tasks that are no longer active in the current API process; it never signals an unverified process after a restart.
- Cancellation preserves the child worktree for review and lets the parent agent continue. A live API integration test starts a delegated child, cancels it by ID during its provider request, verifies the child request aborts, and confirms the parent completes its next model turn. Focused API/client/subagent tests pass (95/95); two full Windows test runs pass (725 total: 706 passed, 19 skipped, zero failures), and the full Ubuntu integration check passes (725 total: 721 passed, four Windows-only skips, zero failures; 205 JavaScript modules syntax-checked).
- Updated API/configuration docs and the orchestration recovery roadmap. At this checkpoint, retry/restart and broader crash-boundary work were still open; retry/restart is implemented in the follow-up above.

## Headless Chrome loopback probe in WSL — 2026-10-23

- The Windows Edge browser smoke passes end-to-end after adding a bounded `/app` HTML preflight, disabling Chrome background networking, and retrying transient profile-file sharing errors while waiting for `DevToolsActivePort`. It verifies the local runtime, accessibility and keyboard navigation, runtime settings, approvals, signed packages, MCP controls, and browser→CLI→browser session-goal continuity. The preflight requires a 200 `text/html` response, the Xyst title, and a body under 2 MiB before browser startup.
- The headless Linux Chrome smoke remains unresolved in this WSL image. Chrome 156 emits `Page.frameStartedLoading` and `Network.requestWillBeSent` for the loopback URL; `strace` confirms its TCP connection to the app port completes (`SO_ERROR` 0), and the same `/app` route passes the Node preflight, but the DevTools `Page.navigate` response never arrives and Chrome reports no network failure. Switching between the pipe and WebSocket debugging transports and disabling background networking do not resolve it. A separate Python loopback server responds to `curl`. This narrows the failure to Chrome's headless navigation/response path in this WSL environment without proving the cause; hosted Ubuntu browser-smoke execution remains pending. The installed Tauri/WebKit Linux window smoke under Xvfb and AT-SPI passes.
- The opt-in live-model smoke now passes end-to-end against the loaded `qwen3-coder-30b-a3b-instruct`: it read both source fixtures, approved and wrote both expected result files, read them back, then ran and verified the exact process and shell markers. The smoke allows five minutes per provider request, a 12-turn cap for the two-file workflow, and a bounded 15-minute run deadline; progress output is opt-in.
- Two full Windows `pnpm check` runs pass (678 passed, 19 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 203 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). The Edge smoke, lint, and format checks pass. No Windows restart is required to use the already-enabled WSL instance.
- One additional Windows full-check repeat ran for 24 minutes and reported two test failures; the app had truncated the test output before their names were retained. An immediately following full check captured to an ignored log passed in about 70 seconds with the same 678/19/0 counts. Treat the slow failure as unresolved intermittent test instability until a captured recurrence identifies the failing cases.

## Central persistence schema registry and memory v2 migration — 2026-10-22

- Added one contiguous, fail-closed schema registry for sessions, compaction archives, project/global memory, goals, and their exports. Memory v1 records and v1 exports migrate to v2; creator provenance is derived from the previous source metadata, while an old update timestamp with no editor metadata is labeled `unknown` rather than guessing. Session and goal records remain v1 and reject unsupported future versions.
- Memory upgrades run under the existing cross-process lock. The store writes and verifies an exact-byte sibling backup before atomically saving the migrated record; if the write is interrupted, it restores the original bytes. Conflicting backups, malformed records, and future versions fail without altering source data. The browser memory import now accepts v1 and v2 exports.
- Added registry-chain, version-refusal, migration, exact-backup, rollback/retry, backup-conflict, malformed-input, export-upgrade, and future-goal-schema tests. The focused persistence/session suite passed 41 tests with four Windows symlink-capability skips. Two full Windows checks each passed 670 tests (19 platform/capability skips, zero failures); two full Ubuntu WSL checks each passed 685 tests (four Windows-only skips, zero failures). Each full check syntax-checked 201 JavaScript modules. Lint, format, and VS Code package checks pass.
- Native Windows rollback behavior when the filesystem refuses atomic replacement, recovery UX for users, and older released session/goal migrations remain open.

Regressions cover multiline CRLF JSON, `event`/`id`/comment fields, and the whitespace-sensitive `[DONE]` marker.

- `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm check:vscode`, and `git diff --check` pass. One earlier elevated Windows run had a single failure that did not reproduce in the two later complete runs. A non-elevated run could not exercise local-loopback API tests and is not counted as platform evidence.

## Global-memory data-directory migration and lock cleanup — 2026-10-22

- Changing `app_data.directory` now copies validated global-memory entries into the selected location, preserves record IDs/provenance, merges disjoint records, refuses duplicate/conflicting source IDs, leaves the prior copy untouched, and writes the destination atomically. The settings API blocks a directory change while API runs or global-memory requests are active; the UI reports copy/already-present counts, and configuration/API/user documentation clarifies that project state, credentials, and extension packages stay put.

## Run-status contract reconciliation — 2026-10-22

- Fixed the shared `AgentRunStatus` union and subagent child-status normalizer to include `invalid_review_output`, which AgentRuntime already emits for malformed Review results. Regressions verify the `run_stopped` event carries the status and delegated results preserve it instead of replacing it with `null`.
- Session status is now typed as active, archived, or a terminal agent status; API run state distinguishes running/paused from terminal agent status, and API finish/failure events have concrete status unions. Immutable runtime status arrays back session persistence validation and subagent child-result normalization. Invalid session statuses are rejected on both save and load. The API reference now lists the status values returned to clients.
- Control API error bodies now have shared response/detail/client contracts. The client preserves valid uppercase server error codes and falls back to `CONTROL_API_ERROR` for absent or malformed codes; the API reference documents the response shape.
- Added a platform-support contract regression tying the target policy to the package engine floor, CI OS/Node matrix, bundled runtime version, and documented architecture/package choices. Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass.

## Windows extended-path display — 2026-10-22

- `displayPath()` now applies the same extended drive/UNC normalization as workspace containment and accepts an explicit path flavor for host-independent tests. Windows drive, UNC, and POSIX display cases pass. The focused path/process suite passes 20 tests with two expected host skips (Windows symlink privilege and POSIX signal semantics); process descendant cancellation and timeout behavior pass on this Windows host.
- Two full Windows checks pass after the path change (613 tests each: 595 passed, 18 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 177 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass.
- `terminateProcessTree()` now skips already-exited children, avoiding stale process-ID/group termination attempts after Node records an exit. A PID-less mock regression verifies the no-signal path safely. The focused path/process suite passes 21 tests with two expected host skips.
- Two full Windows checks pass after the process change (614 tests each: 596 passed, 18 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 177 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass.

## Trusted Windows PowerShell resolution — 2026-10-22

- Native shell scripts, Windows image resizing, and Windows Credential Manager no longer launch `powershell.exe` through PATH lookup. They use a shared resolver that accepts only a local absolute `SystemRoot` or `WINDIR`, rejecting UNC and relative roots; callers fail with an explicit error if no trusted path is available. Resolver regressions verify PATH is ignored. Focused process/tools/image/secret suites pass 45 tests with two expected host skips.
- Two full Windows checks pass after the executable-resolution change (615 tests each: 597 passed, 18 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 177 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass.

## Provider request timeout classification — 2026-10-22

- OpenAI-compatible request timeouts now use the existing `REQUEST_TIMEOUT` code while waiting for headers and while reading JSON or SSE bodies. Caller cancellation remains `CANCELLED`. Model fallback eligibility and agent-loop behavior are unchanged.
- The authenticated `GET /v1/models` integration test confirms that `REQUEST_TIMEOUT` reaches clients while credentials and provider error details remain redacted.
- Provider tests pass 23/23. Two full Windows checks each pass 595 tests (577 passed, 18 platform/capability skips, zero failures) and syntax-check 175 JavaScript modules. Linux-native checks and the existing Windows symlink-capability skips remain platform validation work.

## Bounded provider response bodies — 2026-10-22

- HTTP error responses from OpenAI-compatible providers now retain at most a 4 KiB diagnostic prefix and cancel the remaining body stream. The existing 500-character displayed-detail cap and provider error codes are unchanged. A 1 MiB regression verifies the prefix limit and cancellation.
- Successful JSON responses now require a readable stream; the parser no longer falls back to unbounded `response.text()` when a custom response has no stream. A regression verifies the fallback is not called.
- Provider tests pass 21/21. Two full Windows checks each pass 593 tests (575 passed, 18 platform/capability skips, zero failures) and syntax-check 175 JavaScript modules. Linux-native checks and the existing Windows symlink-capability skips remain platform validation work.

## Provider stream truncation and cancellation handling — 2026-10-22

- Fixed a stream-completion gap: a clean transport close after partial content but before a model finish event was previously reported as success. The provider now rejects that truncated stream as `INVALID_STREAM`; it still accepts `[DONE]` or clean EOF after a finish event. A mid-stream abort after emitted partial text maps to `CANCELLED`.
- Provider tests pass 19/19, including each terminal case. Two full Windows checks each passed 563 tests (546 passed, 17 platform/capability skips, zero failures) and syntax-checked 174 JavaScript modules. Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass; native Linux conformance remains outstanding.

## Provider one-byte SSE boundary fixture — 2026-10-22

- Added a provider regression that feeds UTF-8 response text, CRLF event delimiters, fragmented tool names/arguments, usage data, and the terminal `[DONE]` frame one byte per transport chunk. This exercises every byte boundary, including inside a multibyte character and CRLF separators.
- Provider tests passed 16/16 before the truncation/cancellation follow-up; the expanded provider suite now passes 19/19 as recorded above. The one-byte UTF-8/CRLF regression remains covered. Native Linux provider behavior remains CI validation work.

## Provider stream rejects empty success responses — 2026-10-22

- The OpenAI-compatible streaming client previously treated a clean HTTP 200 with an empty body, only SSE keep-alives, or `[DONE]` before any response event as an empty successful assistant response. It now fails with `INVALID_STREAM`; malformed payloads and provider error events also fail without exposing provider-supplied error text. SSE framing now detects LF, CRLF, and CR delimiters even when a delimiter is split across transport chunks. Added provider regressions and corrected an observability API test that incorrectly returned JSON from a streaming endpoint; the new validation exposed that stale mock.
- Provider tests pass 15/15; the corrected API observability integration passes. Two final full Windows checks each passed 533 tests (516 passed, 17 explicit platform/capability skips, zero failures) and syntax-checked 164 JavaScript modules. Repository lint and format checks plus VS Code package validation pass; lint reports seven informational suggestions and no errors. Native Linux provider behavior still requires CI/device verification.

## Streaming file-tool line ranges — 2026-10-22

- `read_file` now decodes files incrementally, retaining only lines in the requested range instead of materializing the entire file as both a string and a line array. It preserves LF/CRLF and trailing-line behavior, checks for binary NUL bytes throughout the file, and enforces the configured byte ceiling against the actual stream as well as the initial file size.
- Added a regression reading a selected range from a greater-than-1-MiB CRLF file, plus invalid range and binary-data-after-range checks. Focused tool tests pass (11 passed, one Windows symlink-capability skip). ## Strict Review findings validation — 2026-10-21

- Review output is now parsed by a dedicated schema module. It rejects the full report if any finding is malformed, has unsupported severity, empty/oversized text, an invalid line, excessive count/size, or an absolute/traversing path. Accepted findings are normalized to workspace-relative slash paths and copied through an allowlist, dropping model-supplied extra fields. This prevents a mixed valid/invalid report from being silently reduced to an empty or partial “clean” result.
- Agent regression verifies mixed output ends as `invalid_review_output`; parser tests cover path normalization, bounds, whole-report refusal, and output-field allowlisting. Native Linux validation remains open.

- Individual session exports now include a redacted snapshot of only the project goals named in `persistent_goal_ids`. Whole-project backups avoid embedding the same goal data into every session because they already carry one project-level goal bundle.
