## Opt-in global user memory — 2026-10-21

- Added a user-level global memory store under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Xyst AI Assistant\.agent\global-memory.json` on Windows and `$XDG_DATA_HOME/xyst/.agent/global-memory.json` on Linux (defaulting to `~/.local/share/xyst`). It reuses the bounded, redacting, locked version-1 memory format; project memory is not moved or merged.
- Global memory defaults off. Only user config, the user-owned runtime settings sidecar, or the user's environment can enable it; project `.xyst.toml` cannot set `memory.global_enabled`. The Runtime settings panel can toggle it with an explicit model-context disclosure. Turning it off stops use while preserving data for later re-enablement or deletion.
- The authenticated API and browser memory editor support separate project/global list, search, add, edit, delete, export, and import operations. Imports validate format and schema, redact secrets, merge additively, and skip duplicates. The CLI supports the same operations for project and enabled global memory. Agent context includes global entries only when enabled, labels them untrusted user-maintained reference data, redacts secrets, and shares the configured context character budget between project and global memory.
- Two full Windows checks passed after the opt-out privacy regression: 505 tests each, 488 passed, 17 platform/capability skips, zero failures; syntax checked 157 JavaScript modules. Focused tests cover default-off behavior, project-config rejection, separate persistence, authenticated CRUD/import/export, CLI project/global round trips, untrusted context, immediate opt-out with on-disk retention, runtime-setting toggling, and creation of a missing user data root. Formatting, lint, VS Code packaging, and whitespace checks pass; lint retains eight informational notices.

## Persisted audit metadata redaction and directory-swap check — 2026-10-21

- Tool-audit records redact credential-like policy-rule and approved-hunk identifiers and bound hunk identifier lengths. Writes reject symlinked state directories and hard-linked log files, exclusively create new logs, use `O_NOFOLLOW` where available, compare the opened file identity, and recheck the audit directory identity immediately before appending. A controlled open-time directory-swap regression covers the check. Node does not provide a portable handle-anchored append boundary here, so a path mutation race remains between the final directory check and the write.
- Linux CI sets `XYST_REQUIRE_LINK_TESTS=true`, requiring symlink, hard-link, and directory-swap fixtures to run. The directory-swap fixture swaps the directory immediately before opening the audit file, so it exercises the post-open identity check on Windows and Linux. The Windows file-symlink fixture remains unavailable under this host's `EPERM` restriction.
- Two full Windows checks after these changes passed at 497 tests each (480 passed, 17 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 156 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Formatting, lint, VS Code package validation, and whitespace checks passed; lint reports eight informational notices. Two final source archive builds were byte-identical. The rebuilt Windows installer passed its isolated install, service/UI startup, same-version reinstall, configuration retention, shutdown, and uninstall smoke. Native Linux sandbox and pidfd execution remain pending Ubuntu CI.

## Linux Debian package inspection regression coverage — 2026-10-21

- Extracted package payload discovery and launcher validation into `scripts/linux-package-inspection.mjs`. Resource discovery does not follow linked directories and is bounded by depth and directory-count limits. Launcher validation requires a real `/usr/bin`, accepts only executable `xyst` launchers resolved inside the extracted package, and rejects external targets.
- Added five Linux-only tests for executable runtime validation, the search-depth bound, linked-directory avoidance, in-payload versus out-of-payload launchers, and linked or absent `/usr/bin`. They are skipped on Windows because executable-bit and Linux symlink semantics differ; the Ubuntu CI full suite is responsible for running them. The host lacks WSL and a Linux container runtime, so their Linux execution is not locally verified.
- Two Windows full checks passed after these additions (497 tests each: 480 passed, 17 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 156 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). A diagnostic direct test run also passed at 497/480/17. One intervening full run had a single non-reproducing failure; the two consecutive project-wrapper runs passed. Formatting, lint, VS Code package validation, and whitespace checks passed; lint retains eight informational notices. Native DEB build/install smoke remains pending Linux CI.

## Linux recovered process-group leader exit handling — 2026-10-21

- Linux recovery now records and verifies the process-group ID alongside the boot ID, PID start time, and command hash. When the original leader is gone, it probes the recorded group only to distinguish absent from still present; a surviving group is reported as unavailable because its numeric ID cannot safely pin that group against reuse. Recovery never signals that unpinned group. A Linux integration regression covers a reaped leader with a surviving descendant; native pidfd execution is still pending Ubuntu CI because this Windows host cannot run it.
- Two consecutive Windows full checks passed after this change: 487 tests each (475 passed, 12 platform/capability skips, zero failures) and 154 JavaScript modules syntax-checked. The focused background-process/process-identity suites passed 12/12 applicable Windows tests; two Linux-only pidfd tests were skipped. Agent and routing evaluations passed 6/6 and 8/8. Two source archive builds matched exactly (221 files, 746,174 bytes; SHA-256 `e8de7182f92cc5e7aff4249edda811afd71c29dc6084686dfdc9e386f9a8cfcf`). Formatting, lint, VS Code package validation, and whitespace checks passed; lint has eight informational notices. Ubuntu execution remains pending.

## Verified Windows recovered process-tree termination — 2026-10-21

- Recovered Windows background-process stops now revalidate the root using its recorded creation time and image hash on an open process handle, then place the root and currently observable descendants into a kill-on-close Job Object. Descendant handles remain pinned while their kernel-reported parent IDs and creation ordering are checked; repeated Toolhelp snapshots catch descendants that appear during assignment. Termination acts on the job, so ordinary `CreateProcess` children created by attached members inherit the same job. If a snapshot or assignment cannot be verified, recovery reports an unknown state instead of falling back to a PID-only tree kill.
- Added a Windows regression that starts a detached child, simulates manager loss, and verifies recovery stops both the root and child. Windows background-process and process-identity suites pass 12/12 applicable tests (the Linux pidfd integration is skipped on Windows). Two full Windows checks pass after the change: 486 tests each (478 passed, 8 platform/capability skips, zero failures) and 154 JavaScript modules syntax-checked. Formatting, lint, VS Code package validation, and whitespace checks pass; lint retains eight informational suggestions. Two source archive builds are byte-identical (221 files; SHA-256 `d8789b9a1e17e4acbb2083727b483db50592f4236a7c561a4861cc2d39efb444`). Native Linux execution remains unverified on this host. Windows processes created through WMI `Win32_Process.Create` do not automatically inherit Job Object membership and remain an edge case for broader recovery validation.

## Asserted Linux nested-user-namespace isolation — 2026-10-21

- Bubblewrap launch arguments now request `--disable-userns` and also pass `--assert-userns-disabled`. Bubblewrap versions supporting the existing Ubuntu baseline expose the assertion flag; if the runtime cannot disable nested user namespaces, setup must fail before the isolated command starts.
- The command-builder regression requires the assertion flag. The Linux integration suite now also attempts a nested `unshare --user` from inside the sandbox and requires it to fail. The focused sandbox unit suite passes 13 tests with two hard-link capability skips (`EPERM`). Two complete Windows project checks after adding the integration regression each passed 485 tests (477 passed, 8 platform/capability skips, zero failures) and syntax-checked 154 JavaScript modules. Formatting and lint pass with eight informational suggestions. Native Linux Bubblewrap execution still needs Ubuntu CI evidence because this Windows host cannot execute Bubblewrap.

## Windows AppContainer protected descendant permission preflight — 2026-10-21

- The AppContainer workspace grant uses an inheritable ACE on the workspace root. Before granting, the native preflight now inspects every descendant security descriptor and rejects entries whose DACL is protected from inheritance; otherwise a nested directory or file could silently miss the intended workspace grant. Permission-inspection failures also stop launch.
- Added a native Windows regression that marks a nested directory and a file with protected DACLs in turn and verifies each workspace scan fails closed with the offending path. The complete native Rust/AppContainer suite passes 21/21 with host ACL access; `cargo fmt -- --check` and strict Clippy pass.
- Two full Windows project checks pass after the change: 485 tests each (477 passed, 8 platform/capability skips, zero failures) and 154 JavaScript modules syntax-checked. Linux Bubblewrap/pidfd validation, filesystem mutation races, broader escape testing, and independent security review remain open.

## Linux sandbox mount-boundary preflight — 2026-10-21

- Required Bubblewrap launch now reads `/proc/self/mountinfo` and rejects nested mount points inside the workspace, including bind mounts that share the root filesystem device ID. This keeps the existing hard-link scan from overlooking inode names hidden behind nested mounts. A device-ID check also catches cross-device nested directories.
- Added deterministic mountinfo regressions for root mounts, sibling-prefix paths, nested bind mounts, malformed metadata, and the cross-device fallback. Focused sandbox tests pass 13 cases with two host capability skips (hard-link creation is denied by this Windows sandbox). Two consecutive full Windows checks pass: 485 tests each (477 passed, 8 platform/capability skips, zero failures) and 154 JavaScript modules syntax-checked. `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm check:vscode`, and `git diff --check` pass; lint reports eight informational suggestions. WSL is not installed on this host, so native Bubblewrap integration remains dependent on Ubuntu CI; race-resistant snapshotting and independent security review remain open.
- With the bundled Python runtime, all 25 desktop automation tests passed, including generated Linux helper syntax, X11/Wayland input, and guaranteed drag-button release behavior. These fixture tests do not replace native Linux desktop or Bubblewrap validation.
- Added `pnpm smoke:live-resume`, an opt-in acceptance flow that starts a local API run, gates the first OpenAI-compatible chat request, queues a pause and user direction, resumes the same run, and checks that the direction was persisted and honored. It checks LM Studio's bounded, read-only model-status endpoint and returns `MODEL_NOT_LOADED` with an actionable message when the selected model is not loaded. ## Provider-generated recovery summaries — 2026-10-21

- Assisted tool runs reserve input tokens for a final no-tools recovery request when a run stops at a token/cost budget or model-turn limit. The summary is grounded in the redacted session transcript, saved with the static stop reason, and charged to run/session/shared usage accounting. A zero input reserve disables the feature; if run/session or shared daily/monthly budget checks cannot cover the call, Xyst AI Assistant keeps the static stop message. Shared quota is reserved atomically just before the summary request, so concurrent runs can consume it first.
- Cost and output capacity are withheld from normal model and compaction calls; delegated work also receives a reduced session-cost allowance. Recovery calls run model lifecycle hooks, emit started/finished/failed events, and expose the partial summary through the existing assistant event stream. Failed provider attempts account their reserved usage conservatively.
- End-to-end tests cover successful recovery and provider-failure fallback, including tool-free requests, transcript evidence, usage, event delivery, and persisted output. Two consecutive full checks passed: 469 tests each (461 passed, 8 platform/capability skips, zero failures) and 148 JavaScript modules syntax-checked. The check runner now limits file-level test concurrency to two. `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm check:vscode`, and `git diff --check` passed; lint reports eight informational suggestions. Native Linux validation remains open.

## Budget-triggered mid-run compaction — 2026-10-21

Output-only caps do not trigger summarization because compaction cannot create output allowance. Lifecycle events identify context, budget, or combined triggers, and the runtime settings summary displays both configured thresholds.

## User-controlled verified application downloads — 2026-10-21

- Runtime settings keep application updates opt-in: disabled, manual check, or startup notification. When the user chooses to download a release, the API rechecks the signed feed and exact sequence/version, rejects redirects, streams the artifact to temporary storage, and verifies its full byte count and SHA-256 before offering a 15-minute, single-use loopback link. Only one package can be staged at a time; staged data is removed after transfer, replacement, expiry, or service shutdown. The installer or package manager is never invoked automatically.
- Added API and UI coverage for authenticated download preparation, stale release refusal, wrong size/checksum rejection, redirect refusal, one-use delivery, plus signed artifact staging and client request validation. ## Rust dependency license policy gate — 2026-10-21

It evaluates `OR`, `AND`, parentheses, and `WITH` expressions; tests cover the expression semantics and the command-line success/failure paths.

Each run printed all four skips by name/reason. The live Rust inventory and allowlist fit could not be verified on this Windows host because Cargo is unavailable; Linux CI is the required validation of the actual locked dependency graph. Third-party notice packaging and full legal review remain open.

Added common path-safe prefix validation and symlink creation handling that skips only for OS permission/capability errors; migrated the path, tool, skill, and audit symlink tests to that helper.

The runner reported every skip by name and reason. The four skips were unavailable Windows symlink creation, Linux-only pidfd and Bubblewrap integrations, and Windows' non-POSIX child termination behavior.

## Native accessible desktop control — 2026-10-20

- Added `computer_control` to the shared runtime. Windows uses Microsoft UI Automation through the system Windows PowerShell 5.1 host; Linux uses pyatspi/AT-SPI and platform input backends. The model can inspect a bounded accessibility tree, then click/type/send validated key combinations only after rechecking the selected control name and path. Assisted calls require approval and redact recognized secrets from returned UI strings; unrestricted calls use the current user environment without Xyst AI Assistant approval. The tool is omitted when process execution is disabled or required sandboxing is active.
- Added schema, environment filtering, shell-payload encoding, assisted-approval, selector validation, and backend error tests. Focused agent/config/tool/desktop tests passed 74/75 (one symlink-permission skip). Live Windows read-only inspection returned bounded UI data; embedded Linux helper passed Python syntax validation. Full project check passed twice: 282 tests per run, 278 passed and 4 Linux-only isolation skips; 108 JavaScript modules syntax-checked each run. VS Code package validation passed. Linux graphical integration, real mouse/key interaction, screenshot capture, and inaccessible/custom-drawn controls remain unverified/open.

### Coordinate-based desktop input — 2026-10-20

- `computer_control` now supports explicit coordinate clicks and vertical scrolling. Windows resolves screen coordinates to accessible UI Automation patterns; Linux uses xdotool mouse input. Coordinate and range checks run before approval, and Assisted approval summaries show the exact coordinates and action. Windows cannot physically click controls lacking usable accessibility patterns.
- Focused desktop/tool tests cover coordinate validation, serialized Windows payloads, fixed Linux argv use, and selector-free coordinate branches. Windows PowerShell 5.1 parsed the generated script; the embedded Linux helper passed Python syntax compilation. Two full project checks passed: 286 tests per run, 282 passed and 4 Linux-only skips; 108 JavaScript modules syntax-checked per run. Live Windows accessibility inspection works; this host did not exercise real Linux GUI input.

### Transient screenshot vision — 2026-10-20

- `computer_control` captures a bounded screenshot using a native Windows host helper, grim on Wayland, or ImageMagick on X11. A screenshot action is rejected before execution unless the selected model profile declares vision. Captures are validated/resized and sent as a transient multimodal user message; only metadata is saved with the tool result, never image bytes.
- Agent tests prove screenshot bytes reach the vision provider but are absent from saved sessions, and verify non-vision profiles cannot execute capture. The native Windows Rust helper was built and exercised live; it returned PNG data without invoking PowerShell or persisting the image. Standalone Windows CLI fallback still uses PowerShell and may be blocked by Defender AMSI. Linux capture needs a graphical host integration check.

## Unrestricted file-size policy — 2026-10-20

- Unrestricted file reads, writes, and edits bypass configured per-file and per-write byte ceilings, matching its machine-wide filesystem and no-app-policy behavior. Hard runtime ceilings now cap reads, edits, and writes at 32 MiB, directory listings at 5,000 entries, and JSON tool results at 1 MB; larger content must be processed in bounded chunks or with shell tools.
- Regression coverage uses configured one-byte limits to confirm unrestricted reads, writes, and edits exceed those limits.

## Unrestricted automation output and listing limits — 2026-10-21

- Unrestricted mode now ignores the configured tool-result byte ceiling, process stdout/stderr capture ceiling, and directory-list entry ceiling. Assisted mode retains each configured/default cap. Large file-read, 150 KiB process-output, and 500+ entry directory tests verify the unrestricted behavior even when the configured limits are tiny.
- Two full `pnpm test` runs pass on Windows (377 tests: 372 passed, 5 platform/capability skips, zero failures). Focused unrestricted automation tests also pass. Formatting, focused lint, and `git diff --check` pass. Provider context/output limits, host resources, explicit cancellation, and process operation timeouts remain in force.

# Implementation checkpoint

## Windows process-tree termination executable resolution — 2026-10-21

- Process timeout and cancellation cleanup now resolves `taskkill.exe` only under an absolute local `SystemRoot`/`WINDIR` system directory. It ignores `PATH`, rejects relative and UNC roots, falls back to a valid `WINDIR`, and kills only the root child if no valid system path is available.
- Added resolver regression tests. The Windows process tests exercised real timeout and cancellation cleanup and confirmed descendants did not survive. Focused tests passed 11/11 with one Windows-specific signal-behavior skip. Two complete repository checks passed: 308 tests each, 304 passed, 4 platform skips, zero failures; 114 JavaScript modules passed syntax checks. `pnpm lint` passed with 10 informational suggestions and no warnings; `pnpm format:check` passed.
- The Windows AppContainer design is recorded in ADR 0010, but its native helper, filesystem/network integration, escape tests, and independent security review remain open.

## Tool execution policies and provider schema translation — 2026-10-21

- Added typed timeout and sandbox metadata to every built-in and normalized MCP tool. Process, shell, background-process, desktop, web-search, semantic-search, subagent, and MCP timeout settings now resolve through that metadata; sandbox audit decisions and desktop-tool exclusion under required isolation use the declared sandbox requirement. Platform filesystem operations remain explicitly marked as operating-system bounded rather than claiming a runtime deadline.
- Direct background-process calls now validate supplied timeout values against the same metadata used for defaults, closing a bypass found during review. Updated the subagent timeout fixture to honor the public 1-second minimum. Provider translation recursively strips the runtime-only `maxBytes` extension from outbound JSON Schema while preserving the local UTF-8 byte checks.
- Focused contract/tool/MCP/desktop/web/semantic/subagent/provider tests passed 50/51 with one explicit Windows symlink-capability skip. A live LM Studio smoke listed 37 models, completed a local chat on `openai/gpt-oss-20b`, and parsed a `send_background_input` tool call without executing it.
- `pnpm format:check` and `pnpm lint` passed (10 informational suggestions, no warnings). Two full project checks passed: 307 tests each, 303 passed and 4 explicit platform skips, zero failures; all 114 JavaScript modules syntax-checked per run.

## Provider response bounds — 2026-10-20

- Added one shared bounded JSON-response reader used by provider chat, provider model listing, and local-server discovery. It enforces declared and streamed byte limits, cancels oversized streams, rejects invalid UTF-8/JSON, and never includes response bodies in its error text.

## Local provider discovery hardening — 2026-10-20

- Hardened configured/default local OpenAI-compatible server discovery. Config accepts at most 16 lowercase aliases; duplicate base URLs are probed once; configured aliases take precedence; each response is capped at 1 MiB and 500 model IDs (256 characters each); redirects are rejected so configured bearer credentials cannot be forwarded; and at most four probes run concurrently. Positive and negative results remain cached for 15 seconds, with an 800 ms default request timeout.
- Added tests for redirect policy, oversized bodies, bounded model identifiers, concurrency, and invalid endpoint counts. All 10 discovery/secret tests passed; the combined discovery/config run passed 23/23.

## File-write diff review and stale-file guard — 2026-10-20

- The shared runtime builds a bounded unified diff and stable hunk ranges for text-file creates/updates. Browser and VS Code approval cards let the user select individual hunks; the API validates selections, disallows lasting grants for partial approvals, and records accepted hunk IDs. The write path reconstructs the selected content from the reviewed proposal and preserves unselected source ranges.
- Before replacement, the runtime verifies the canonical target path and content hash against the reviewed snapshot; stale edits fail without overwriting newer contents. Binary, oversized, invalid UTF-8, or very fragmented changes fall back to whole-action approval with a clear preview limitation. Persistent conversation-wide review of completed changes remains open.

## Explicit routing fallback and diagnostics — 2026-10-20

- Added an optional strict `routing.fallback_chain`. The capable configured default remains first; chain entries follow in order, and omitted profiles are excluded from automatic selection. Explicit user model choices remain strict and never silently switch.
- With a strict chain configured and automatic model selection, the runtime probes each eligible endpoint's `/models` list in priority order, requires the configured model ID, and skips failed/unavailable endpoints before sending the user prompt. Probe timeout is configurable. Explicit, plan, and review model selections bypass probing and remain strict.
- Routing decisions now persist required context/tool/vision factors, availability results, and eligible/rejected alternatives with capability/provider reasons; the run-start event carries the same decision for clients. Before any streamed text is delivered, known connection refusal/name-resolution failures, HTTP 429, and model-not-found errors can advance to the next chain entry after rechecking availability. Rate-limit input is charged conservatively and failed output reservations are released. Other provider errors and model switching after an accepted turn remain unimplemented. A remote profile in the explicit chain may receive prompts when earlier profiles are unavailable.
- Live LM Studio end-to-end runs verified model-list preflight and successful `HEALTHY` completions. They exposed a small-output-budget bug where the default 512-token reserve left one token for work; the default now scales to at most one quarter of the available output cap (maximum 512), with explicit reserve configuration unchanged.
- Two full `node scripts/check.mjs` runs passed (218 tests, 214 passed, 4 platform skips each; 91 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). VS Code package validation, routing evaluation (8/8), example TOML parsing, release archive build, and whitespace checks passed after the final code changes.

CI builds the archive on Windows/Linux and package tests compare byte-for-byte builds and verify archived contents and hashes.

## Budget enforcement milestone

- Per-session cumulative token and estimated-cost ceilings are enforced before provider calls. Daily/monthly token and cost ceilings use a shared cross-process ledger with conservative in-flight reservations, atomic updates, stale reservation recovery, and CLI status visibility (`usage status` and interactive `/budget`). Cost ceilings fail closed when model pricing is unknown.
- Ledger coverage includes concurrent reservation races, settlement to actual usage, month boundaries, unknown pricing, expired reservation accounting, and agent-level no-provider-call on exhausted daily budget.

- `final_response_reserve_tokens` withholds output allowance from tool-capable provider calls. When the working allowance is exhausted, the runtime preserves tool observations and appends a partial-result explanation; it refuses proposed tool calls that would consume the reserve. The separately configured recovery-summary input reserve and protected output allowance now fund a no-tools partial summary when remaining run/session/shared quotas permit it.

Recent turns are retained. Tests cover successful archive/usage accounting and shared-budget refusal before provider contact. Context- and budget-threshold triggers now run between model turns.

## Earlier verified snapshot

- Optional Brave web search now runs through the shared tool and approval policy. It is disabled by default; approved queries use an environment or OS-vault credential reference, requests/results are bounded, only HTTPS result links are returned, cancellation and rate limits apply, and page data is persisted as untrusted observations. CLI/API authorization, denial-with-zero-network-call, secret non-disclosure, response-size bounds, and settings sanitization have deterministic tests. No live Brave request was made because no search credential was configured.
- Delegated subagents now require parent approval, run with a fresh transcript in an isolated managed Git worktree, receive no parent grants, ask separately for writes/processes, and keep changes unmerged for review. The task store records status/results and worktree paths with cross-process capacity locks; CLI/API readers expose history. Child usage is aggregated against the parent token/cost totals. Crash recovery, stale lock repair, and task-ID cancellation remain open.
- Two consecutive final-tree `node scripts/check.mjs` runs passed: 144 tests, 140 passed, 4 platform skips; syntax checked for 69 JavaScript modules. Both runs used local loopback access for API integration.
- Image input is implemented across CLI (`--image`), authenticated API, and browser attachment flow. PNG/JPEG/WebP content is detected and bounded by count, bytes, and pixels; oversized inputs are resized to metadata-stripped JPEG, vision capability is required, and only image metadata is persisted. Windows real resizing and a live LM Studio Qwen-VL synthetic red-image prompt both passed. Ubuntu CI now installs ImageMagick for the real Linux resize test; that runner has not executed yet.
- Two consecutive final-tree `node scripts/check.mjs` runs passed: 137 tests, 133 passed, 4 platform skips; syntax checked for 67 JavaScript modules. Both runs used loopback access for local API tests. `git diff --check` passed with only the known Git line-ending normalization warning for `BUILD_ROADMAP.md`.
- Offline routing evaluation harness and 8 labeled synthetic regression cases are implemented; `npm run eval:routing` reports overall and per-category selection accuracy with a failing exit code for mismatches.
- Two consecutive `node scripts/check.mjs` runs passed after CLI/API/browser session export/import landed: 130 tests, 126 passed, 4 platform skips; syntax checked for 64 JavaScript modules. The four skips include Windows-only tests unavailable on Linux and Linux-only Bubblewrap/pidfd integration cases unavailable on this Windows host. API integration coverage confirms that imports above the general 1 MiB JSON limit work within the dedicated 50 MiB session-export limit. `git diff --check` reports only Git's existing line-ending normalization warning for `BUILD_ROADMAP.md`.
- The suite needs loopback permission for local API/provider integration tests; runs in the restricted shell fail with `EACCES` and pass when allowed to bind/connect to their local test servers.
- The synthetic routing labels test configured deterministic policy behavior, not real user task quality. Native desktop/IDE packaging, production retrieval/budget features, Linux validation on Linux, and other remaining roadmap work are still outstanding.
- Background process lifecycle now has persistent UUID records, session-scoped model-tool access, bounded/redacted output, explicit approval for model starts/stops, timeout handling, audited authenticated API/CLI stop/removal, browser-panel controls, and API client methods. Live records owned by another manager are marked unmanaged and refreshed. After owner exit, persisted Linux boot ID/start ticks/argv hash or Windows process creation time/image hash are checked before recovery; PID reuse and unavailable identity fail closed. Linux recovered stops target the verified process group; Windows recovered stops terminate only the verified root process. Native desktop integration and full Windows descendant recovery remain open.
- Windows recovered termination rechecks creation time and image hash on an open process handle before terminating it; its integration test passed on this host. Linux recovered termination opens a pidfd, rechecks boot ID/start ticks/argv hash while the PID is pinned, then signals the process group before releasing the handle. Zombie leaders remain verifiable by boot ID/start ticks so their original group can be stopped while the leader PID is still held by the kernel. Both paths fail closed on identity mismatch; Linux additionally requires Python 3.9+ and Linux 5.3+ pidfd support. If the group leader has already been reaped, descendants cannot currently be safely identified. The Linux integration test is added but was skipped on this Windows host; Ubuntu CI now installs Python 3 and requires the pidfd test to run, but that workflow has not yet executed. Descendant-safe Windows recovery remains unfinished.
- The full check initially exposed approval-rule lock contention under suite load. The default bounded acquisition wait is now 10 seconds (previously 2 seconds); two subsequent full runs passed, including concurrent writer coverage.

## Implemented

- Dependency-free ESM project scaffold, cross-platform CI, architecture docs, security model, and runtime contracts.
- TOML parser for user/project config layers, environment overrides, URL and credential-reference validation, and Windows/Linux config path selection. It supports quoted keys, tables, nested/multiline arrays, inline tables, arrays of tables, scalar values, validated date/time tokens, and multiline strings. Full TOML 1.0 conformance remains unverified.
- Workspace path containment, symlink target checks, long-path/UNC lexical handling, direct argv process runner, filtered child environment, timeout escalation, cancellation, and bounded output. Timeouts/cancellation terminate descendant processes using POSIX process groups and Windows `taskkill /T /F` with direct-child fallback.
- OpenAI-compatible HTTP provider, model listing, chat and SSE streaming, normalized errors, local endpoint discovery/cache, and capability-aware routing.
- Tool-calling agent loop, approval policy, read-only review/plan modes, per-turn input/output budgets, whole-turn context trimming, user-visible events, and persistent session records.
- Workspace read/list/search/write/edit/process tools. Sensitive paths are blocked, credential-like output is redacted, file writes have size limits, and process execution is opt-in. It can run unsandboxed or through the optional Linux isolation backend.
- Optional Linux Bubblewrap process isolation with a restricted mount namespace, private `/tmp`, cleared environment, network/PID/user namespace separation, and fail-closed required mode. Windows required mode fails closed pending a native backend.
- Versioned session CRUD, compaction with immutable older transcript archive, recap, project memory, persistent project goals, and ignore-aware keyword/path retrieval.
- CLI for interactive/non-interactive tasks, review/plan, sessions, compaction, memory, goals, model discovery, search, and OS-vault credential management. CLI and API approvals support one-time, exact session action, exact workspace path for one hour, and exact argv for one hour; wildcard-bearing commands cannot request an exact command grant.
- Session exports are versioned JSON with included compaction archives. CLI, authenticated API, and browser UI export/import are bounded to 50 MiB; imports receive fresh IDs and clear grants, worktree paths, and budget state. Structured and secret-like text redaction runs on exported session/archive content. This is pattern-based redaction, not a guarantee that arbitrary user content contains no secrets.
- Authenticated loopback control API with run lifecycle, bounded/replayable SSE events, remote approval responses, cancellation, session CRUD and grant-management endpoints, paginated project-memory endpoints, goal CRUD/status endpoints, per-process ephemeral bearer tokens, explicit browser-origin allowlisting, Host/peer validation, request limits, and CLI `serve` launcher.
- Authenticated API workspace retrieval at `GET /v1/retrieval/search`, with bounded query/result parameters, existing sensitive-path and ignore handling, redacted excerpts, and per-match source/file freshness metadata. The API client exposes `searchWorkspace()` for desktop and IDE consumers.
- Authenticated read-only settings snapshot at `GET /v1/settings`; it uses an explicit allowlist and reports credential presence without exposing credential values, environment variable names, or OS-vault keys.
- Dependency-free local browser UI served at `/app` from the loopback API, with session history and rename/fork/archive/delete actions, streaming agent/plan/review runs, approval cards, model selection, keyword search, project memory/goal editing, and a read-only settings panel. The shell uses a strict CSP, rejects cross-site loading, and renders user/workspace text without HTML parsing.
- Authenticated `GET /v1/models` queries configured providers with bounded concurrency, per-request timeouts, identifier caps, safe error codes, and redirect rejection; vault credentials stay within the provider request.
- Model-list requests propagate client disconnect cancellation to upstream provider fetches. Provider redirects fail rather than forwarding credentials to another origin.
- Loopback-only API client with redirect rejection, typed response errors, run/session operations, incremental SSE parsing, and stream cancellation when consumers stop early.
- Per-session cross-process lock files and revision checks prevent concurrent CLI/API saves from silently replacing newer session state. Lock contention and stale revisions return actionable `SESSION_LOCKED` / `SESSION_CONFLICT` errors.
- Append-only structured tool audit records capture redacted arguments, policy and approval decisions, sandbox status, duration, and outcome. File payloads and tool results are omitted; records use restrictive permissions and reject symlink destinations.
- One-time approvals and expiring, session-scoped exact-action grants are available in the CLI and API. Grants hash canonical tool arguments, expire within 24 hours, are capped at 100 active grants per session, are cleared on fork, can be revoked, and write grant creation/revocation audit events.
- Configurable tool denials, canonical workspace path globs, and argv command patterns support deny and auto-approve rules; denies take precedence over allows and grants.
- Workspace managed rules can be created, listed, and revoked through the CLI or authenticated API; tool, path-glob, and command-glob rules support allow/deny decisions and persisted 60-second to 30-day expiry, with creation/revocation audit records.
- Windows Credential Manager and Linux Secret Service adapters implement the secret-store interface without plaintext fallback. Models can reference OS-vault entries with `api_key_secret`; provider credentials resolve at request time. CLI migration reads credentials from environment variables without accepting values in argv or echoing them. Windows set/read/list/delete was exercised against the live vault. Linux set/get/list/delete passed against an isolated GNOME Keyring in Ubuntu WSL; key listing reads item attributes through D-Bus and never requests credential payloads. Native Ubuntu Desktop login-session behavior remains open.
- Tool results, denials, validation failures, and errors are stored and returned to the model in a typed `untrusted_observation` envelope carrying source tool and call ID.
- Hierarchical `AGENTS.md` files load from the workspace root through the directory chain for file targets. Content is size-bounded, redacted, marked as untrusted, and cannot override runtime security policy. The first action on a newly scoped path is deferred until the model receives the applicable guidance.
- Ctrl+C cancels agent runs during provider work or a pending interactive approval; the runtime interrupts approval callbacks even if they do not resolve, and the interactive CLI returns to its prompt.
- Git worktrees can be created/listed/removed through the CLI and authenticated API under `.agent/worktrees`; runs can select them and saved sessions resume in the associated registered worktree. Worktree runs use the selected checkout while sharing project memory, goals, approvals, and audit state from the root workspace. Removal refuses tracked, untracked, ignored files, and active cross-process leases. Git hooks/fsmonitor/configured checkout filters are disabled during creation. Switching remains open; crashed leases fail closed and need verified manual cleanup.
- CLI runs can select managed worktrees with `--worktree-id`; an associated saved session automatically resumes in its registered worktree. CLI runs acquire the same cross-process lease as API runs, load the selected worktree's project configuration, and retain root project state. A live mock-provider integration verifies session persistence and lease release.
- New sessions receive a redacted, bounded title from the first user request; explicit session renames are normalized and secret-redacted before persistence. User memory/goal writes are redacted, and existing stored values are redacted again before model-context assembly. Session summaries expose titles for the browser client and CLI.
- Workspace app state under `.agent/` is now protected from direct file and directory tools, including when a broad approval glob is configured.
- Automated syntax/test runner and Windows/Linux GitHub Actions matrix.
- Test suite covering config, context, paths, processes/timeouts, providers/SSE, discovery, secret filtering, retrieval, tools, API auth/origin/Host checks and approvals, sessions/compaction, goals/memory, CLI, and mocked end-to-end agent runs.

- Complete supported-platform AppContainer/Bubblewrap escape and crash-recovery matrices, broader injection/privacy review, torn-write and orphan cleanup coverage, and independent security and legal review. Keep the RustSec GLib unsoundness warning visible until the supported dependency stack changes or review accepts it.
- Resolve or explicitly scope out WMI/out-of-tree process creation for recovered Windows background jobs; ordinary nested descendants created during recovery now have a passing race integration. Continue broader process, sandbox, and temporary-artifact crash-boundary coverage.
- Finish representative large-repository retrieval and routing quality evaluation, concurrent budget-overrun validation, provider-specific prompt-cache controls, broader task categories, and remaining recovery/orphan-management behavior.
- Complete production release operations: protected application/extension signing, hosted feed and key custody/rotation, cross-version update/rollback tests, clean-machine package matrix, artifact attestations/SBOM, and final release documentation.
- Close remaining CLI/desktop/VS Code parity and accessibility evidence, including native host pairing, reconnect/reload failure recovery, screen-reader order, high-DPI/multi-monitor behavior, and physical Linux window-system testing. See the ordered gates in [BUILD_ROADMAP.md](../BUILD_ROADMAP.md) for the full checklist.

## Shared usage-budget concurrency regression — 2026-10-25

- Added a cross-process regression for the shared daily reservation ledger. Eight independent Node processes race to reserve 10 input tokens each under a 30-token cap; exactly three succeed, and the resulting ledger retains all three reservations without exceeding the cap.
- `node --test tests/usage-budget.test.mjs` passed twice on Windows (11/11 each run). On Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4, the same 11 tests also passed twice; the temporary test copy was removed afterward. The eight-process race passed in all four runs. Biome check passed for the changed test. End-to-end overrun validation across providers/platforms remains open.

## Offline routing evaluation milestone

- Added a dependency-free deterministic routing evaluator and synthetic labeled dataset. It reports overall and per-category accuracy, explicit mismatches, malformed/duplicate fixture errors, and a nonzero CLI exit on failure; it never calls a provider or records credentials.
- Cases exercise quality/cost/speed strategy, local preference, context/tool capability filtering, unknown prices, and explicit model overrides. This is a regression harness, not evidence that model choices fit real user tasks; labeled task outcomes and end-to-end scenario evaluation remain open.
- Run with `npm run eval:routing` or `node scripts/evaluate-routing.mjs <dataset.json>`.

## Isolated subagent milestone

- Added opt-in `delegate_task` with explicit approval, a fresh child session, and a dedicated managed Git worktree based on the current commit. The child receives no parent transcript or session grants, cannot delegate recursively, and has token, turn, timeout, and concurrency bounds. Project memory remains shared read-only through the existing context path; child writes and process use still follow normal approval policy.
- Persistent task records redact secrets, bound retained history, expose authenticated list/read API and CLI inspection, and retain child worktrees for review or diagnosis. Child usage is added to parent totals. Parent cancellation and timeout propagate to the child; failures and completion states remain inspectable.
- In Assisted mode, child runs receive at most 40% of the parent’s currently available session-cost allowance; the focused regression verifies the allowance passed into child execution. It passed twice on Windows and twice on Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4. Unrestricted mode continues to omit the configured child session-cost cap.
- Two runs of `node scripts/check.mjs`: 150 tests total, 146 passed, 4 platform skips; syntax checked for 72 JavaScript modules. `git diff --check` passed with the existing Git line-ending normalization warning for `BUILD_ROADMAP.md`. Routing evaluation passed 8/8 cases; example configuration loaded with subagents and web search disabled by default.
- Remaining: merge/rebase workflow, malformed/legacy lock recovery, broader Linux and adversarial validation. Authenticated task-ID cancellation, explicit retry with attempt lineage and a fresh worktree, and read-only interrupted-task/worktree inspection are implemented; retry and cancellation flows have focused regressions.

## Semantic retrieval milestone

- Added OpenAI-compatible embeddings with bounded batches, request timeout/cancellation, redirect rejection, response-size caps, vector shape/order/dimension checks, and optional environment/vault credentials.
- Added opt-in incremental workspace vectors with deterministic `.gitignore` and generated-directory exclusions, sensitive path and binary rejection, secret redaction before embeddings, 64 MiB scan bound, 32 MiB/5,000 chunk index cap, hash-based refresh/deletion, cosine ranking, and live source excerpts with line ranges. Persistent index records hold vectors and metadata, not source content.
- The directory-swap race test replaces a discovered workspace folder with an outside link and confirms no canary reaches embeddings. It passes on Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 with a symlink and on Windows with a junction when run with filesystem link privileges; ordinary sandboxed Windows runs may report EPERM and skip it. Residual parent-directory race review remains open.
- Line-level search used by CLI, API, and `search_files` now reads from bounded regular-file handles, rejects symlink/reparse escapes and path-identity changes, and stops after 64 MiB of file data with an explicit `truncated` result. A directory-swap test confirms outside canaries never reach results on Ubuntu WSL or Windows junctions. Semantic retrieval now fuses exact standalone line matches with semantic chunks, merges line hits already covered by a chunk, and returns exact matches outside the bounded vector chunk set using the already-scanned documents, with no additional filesystem pass. The 13 retrieval/index tests pass on Windows and Ubuntu WSL ext4; the retrieval/tool suite passes on Windows (21 passed, one expected POSIX-only skip), and the authenticated semantic-search API test passes. Watcher updates, large-repository measurement, and residual parent-directory race review remain open.
- Added `semantic_search` tool behind explicit approval, remote HTTPS/privacy configuration gates, `search --semantic`, `index status|clear`, API settings visibility without exposing endpoint or credential references, and a GUI search toggle with a confirmation prompt. Authenticated `POST /v1/retrieval/semantic` rejects requests without consent, audits approved searches, and propagates client disconnect cancellation.
- Focused semantic/provider/config/runtime/CLI/API tests passed: 57/57. Two runs of `node scripts/check.mjs` passed: 156 tests total, 152 passed, 4 platform skips; syntax checked for 77 JavaScript modules. Routing evaluation passed 8/8; the example TOML loaded with semantic retrieval disabled by default. `git diff --check` passed with the existing `BUILD_ROADMAP.md` line-ending normalization warning.
- LM Studio loopback `/v1/models` responded, but `/v1/embeddings` returned HTTP 400 `No models loaded`; live vector quality could not be tested because no embedding model was loaded. Deterministic mock vectors verify indexing and ranking behavior.
- At the time of this 2026-10-22 entry, background watcher invalidation and scale testing remained open. Current status: the API semantic index has recursive watcher-backed in-memory content caching with periodic full refresh and uncached failure fallback; the 2,400-file Windows/WSL benchmark and a 24-file actual save-burst regression pass. Large burst/overflow soak, crash recovery, repository-scale evaluation, and durable vector-store workload comparison remain open. The current vector cache is bounded atomic JSON.

## Session budget milestone

- Preserved per-run input/output token ceilings and added optional cumulative session input/output token caps across resumed runs. Child token usage continues to aggregate into the parent session usage.
- Added an optional USD session cost ceiling. Calls reserve against a conservative UTF-8 request-byte prompt bound plus provider `max_tokens`, using configured input/output rates, and settle from provider token counts or estimated counts. Cost-limited calls fail closed when model prices or historical session cost are unknown. The API settings snapshot and GUI show configured session ceilings.
- Focused agent/config tests passed 33/33, including resumed-session exhaustion and pre-call cost reservation. Full checks after these budget edits remain to be run twice.
- The shared daily/monthly reservation ledger, concurrent-run accounting, and daily/monthly usage surfaces are now implemented: the authenticated `/v1/usage` API and desktop Settings show aggregate totals and attribution, while interactive CLI `/usage` and `/budget` expose the same ledger data. Eight independent processes racing under one daily cap are covered on Windows and Ubuntu WSL ext4. Remaining: external billing reconciliation and provider-specific end-to-end usage/overrun validation. Price-cap guarantees still depend on configured model price metadata and providers honoring `max_tokens`.

## Resume order

1. Run `node scripts/check.mjs` and `git diff --check` after any code changes.
2. Fix failures before extending features.
3. Work through `BUILD_ROADMAP.md` in dependency order. Keep the CLI and runtime as the reference client until the local API contract is stable.
4. Update this checkpoint after each verified milestone so work can continue after context compaction.

## Hybrid semantic retrieval milestone — 2026-10-20

- Semantic search now combines embedding similarity with exact lexical evidence over indexed chunks using reciprocal-rank fusion. This preserves semantic matches while allowing exact identifiers and terminology to outrank embedding-only ties. Results retain the existing bounded excerpts, path filters, source metadata, and privacy controls; stored index records still contain no source text.
- Added deterministic coverage proving exact lexical matches win an embedding-score tie and updated the authenticated API source-label assertion. Focused semantic index tests passed 4/4.
- Two full `node scripts/check.mjs` runs passed: 219 tests total, 215 passed, 4 platform skips; 91 JavaScript modules syntax-checked. Linux-only Bubblewrap and pidfd integration checks remain unexecuted on this Windows host and are required in Linux CI.
- Routing evaluation, VS Code package validation, TOML example parse, and source archive build passed at the following lifecycle milestone. Remaining retrieval work includes background indexing, Linux/large-repository soak, crash recovery, and measured search quality.

## Semantic index lifecycle controls — 2026-10-20

- Added CLI `index reindex` for a full cache rebuild and `index exclude list|add|remove` for persistent workspace-relative glob exclusions. Invalid traversal, absolute paths, negation patterns, and oversized rules are rejected. Exclusions are applied during scans and stale cached entries are removed even when a scan hits its cap.
- Added lifecycle tests for immediate exclusion, path traversal rejection, rebuild recovery after an embedding failure, and rebuilding after removing an exclusion. Focused CLI/index tests passed 15/15.
- Fixed a process finalization race found during repeat verification: stop now waits for the process record's final persisted state, and `settle()` lets teardown wait for queued child-output/exit writes before removing temporary storage.
- After that fix, two full `node scripts/check.mjs` runs passed: 221 tests total, 217 passed, 4 platform skips; 91 JavaScript modules syntax-checked each run.

## API and browser semantic-index management — 2026-10-20

- Added authenticated API routes for index status, explicit reindex, clear, and exclusion list/add/remove. Index and exclusion operations write audit events. Reindex requires an approval flag and cancels when the client disconnects; the index swap remains atomic so embedding failures retain the previous cache.
- Added API client methods and browser settings controls for index status/rebuild/clear and exclusion management. The browser names the configured local or remote embedding destination before reindex confirmation.
- Focused API/client/index tests passed 41/41. Two full `node scripts/check.mjs` runs passed: 222 tests total, 218 passed, 4 platform skips; 91 JavaScript modules syntax-checked each run. Linux-only integration tests remain dependent on Linux CI.

## Opt-in automatic retrieval context — 2026-10-20

- Added `retrieval.auto_context` (off by default) requiring enabled semantic retrieval. Each configured run performs one hybrid top-k lookup, emits content-minimized audit events with a prompt hash, and injects only bounded excerpts as transient untrusted context. Failed retrieval does not prevent the model run; cancellation is honored.
- Automatic indexing caps fresh work at 16 chunks and 250 KB scanned per run. Context defaults to four results and 8,000 characters, with validated user-config limits. Context trimming drops retrieval before dropping the active request; snippets are not written to session history.
- Added tests for configuration validation, ranking/index work caps, context overflow priority, runtime privacy/audit behavior, and graceful retrieval failure. Focused agent/context/config/index tests passed 65/65.
- Two full `node scripts/check.mjs` runs passed: 226 tests each, 222 passed, 4 platform skips; 91 JavaScript modules syntax-checked each run. Linux-only Bubblewrap/pidfd checks still require the Linux CI runner.

## Cross-process project-memory mutation safety — 2026-10-21

- Memory append, update, and delete now acquire an exclusive `.agent/memory.lock` before re-reading and atomically replacing the JSON document. Lock acquisition has a bounded wait; contention or a stale lock reports `MEMORY_LOCKED` without reading lock contents into logs/errors and fails closed instead of silently overwriting another writer. The recovery instruction requires verifying the owner is stopped before manually removing a stale lock.
- Lock cleanup now checks the open file's device/inode identity and removes the path only if it still refers to that exact lock, so a replacement created by another writer is not deleted during teardown.
- Memory reads now cap the input at 4 MiB before parsing; stored text is capped at 10,000 characters and each project is limited to 1,000 entries. Entry timestamps, IDs, and provenance are validated. Save-side size checks use the exact formatted JSON size. Linked `.agent` directories and memory files are rejected, and opened file identity is checked against the pre-open file metadata.
- Added tests for eight concurrent independent stores, six separate Node processes writing the same project, replacement-lock preservation, lock contention preserving the original document, oversized input, malformed entry metadata, and linked-directory rejection. Focused tests passed (15 passed, one symlink-capability skip). Two full project checks passed with 314 tests each (310 passed, 4 platform-specific skips, no failures). Linux filesystem lock semantics remain unverified on this Windows host.
## Versioned project-memory portability — 2026-10-21

- Added `xyst-memory` export/import envelopes, authenticated API/client methods, and Export/Import controls in the browser memory panel. Exports pattern-redact secret-like values. The browser previews the item count and explains future untrusted-context use before importing. Imports validate export and schema versions before mutation, validate entry bounds, assign fresh IDs and `import` provenance, preserve creation timestamps, merge additively, and skip same-section duplicate text so repeated imports are idempotent. All imported entries are prepared and limit-checked before one locked atomic save.
- Added store tests for redaction, fresh IDs, provenance, duplicate imports, unsupported versions, and atomic rejection when an import exceeds the project-wide item cap or byte ceiling; added client and authenticated API integration coverage. Focused store/client tests passed 45/46 with one expected symlink capability skip; the live loopback API portability test passed 1/1. Two full project checks passed with 318 tests each (314 passed, 4 platform-specific skips, no failures); all 114 JavaScript modules passed syntax checks. Lint and format checks pass with nine informational suggestions and no warnings/errors. Linux-only sandbox and pidfd behavior remains unverified on this Windows host.

## Windows AppContainer launch diagnosis — 2026-10-21

- Reproduced the existing `CreateProcessW` error 87 on this Windows host and isolated it to using a hand-built or API-returned package-local path as the child's `LOCALAPPDATA` value. Replaced hand-built path discovery with `GetAppContainerFolderPath` for the private temp directory, kept the explicit caller-provided `LOCALAPPDATA`, and route `TEMP`/`TMP` to the AppContainer folder.
- The native Windows test now launches `cmd.exe` in a no-capability AppContainer, verifies it can create a file under `%TEMP%`, checks the exit status, and removes the temporary folder and profile. The focused live Windows test passes. This only validates launch and private-temp access; no workspace ACL, isolation escape, cancellation, stdio, helper packaging, or Xyst AI Assistant runtime integration is implemented. Required sandbox mode remains fail-closed.

## Windows AppContainer workspace access prototype — 2026-10-21

- The native launcher now validates the selected workspace path, confines the working directory to it, opens the root without following reparse points, captures its volume/file identity, and rejects workspaces containing reparse points or files with multiple hard links. It adds a unique AppContainer SID ACE with read/write/execute and deletion rights plus file/directory inheritance to the workspace root; Windows propagates the ACE to existing descendants. Cleanup rereads the current DACL and revokes only that unique SID's ACE, preserving unrelated DACL entries. The ACE is protected by an in-memory cleanup guard during normal errors.
- Expanded the live Windows test: the container reads a pre-existing workspace file, writes a new file in the workspace, and writes to private `%TEMP%`; the root and existing-file DACL bytes are identical to their before-run snapshots after cleanup. A deterministic test covers hard-link-count rejection. All 9 Rust desktop tests pass; Rust formatting and strict Clippy pass.
- This prototype is still not safe for production integration: ACL crash recovery journaling is absent, root DACL propagation can skip unavailable/protected descendants and is not verified across arbitrary trees, and network, escape, timeout, cancellation, stdio, helper packaging, and Node runtime integration remain open. Required sandbox stays fail-closed.

## Windows AppContainer ACL crash recovery — 2026-10-21

- Before granting a unique AppContainer SID access to the workspace, the launcher now writes and flushes a versioned recovery record in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Xyst AI Assistant\SandboxRecovery`, then commits the record with a write-through rename. An exclusive no-share lock file serializes recovery and sandbox runs; the OS releases the handle if the host exits.
- The kill-on-close Job Object is now configured before process creation and included in `PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_JOB_LIST`; Windows creates the suspended child inside the job, eliminating the separate-creation/assignment crash window.
- Startup recovery validates bounded record size/schema, profile-name format, derived SID equality, canonical workspace path, and the recorded volume/file identity. It revokes only that SID from the current DACL, removes the temporary profile, and deletes the journal last. Malformed records, changed targets, ACL errors, or lock contention block launch and preserve the record.
- Added a live Windows interrupted-grant simulation proving root and existing-child DACL restoration, an identity-mismatch test proving recovery refuses to alter permissions and retains its journal, malformed-record retention coverage, and lock-exclusion coverage. `cargo test --locked` passed twice (13/13 each); strict Clippy and Rust formatting passed. The live process test exercises atomic job-list assignment; force-killing a helper at every journal/ACL/profile transition and arbitrary-tree DACL propagation remain release gates. Required Windows sandbox mode remains fail-closed and is not runtime-integrated.
- The non-test Windows desktop binary also builds successfully with `cargo build --locked` after the process-creation attribute change.

## Windows AppContainer timeout and cancellation — 2026-10-21

- `run_appcontainer_command` now requires a finite timeout and accepts an optional host-owned cancellation event. A pre-signaled event terminates the suspended child before it runs; during execution, a multi-object wait observes the process and cancellation handle. Timeout/cancellation terminates the kill-on-close Job Object and returns exit code, elapsed time, and distinct stop flags.
- Added live Windows tests for an active timeout, pre-resume cancellation, cancellation while the child is running, and timeout bound validation. Together with recovery and workspace tests, `cargo test --locked` passed twice (17/17 each); strict Clippy and Rust formatting pass. Native stdio capture/forwarding, wiring cancellation from the Node/Tauri host, trusted helper packaging, Linux CI, force-kill recovery transition testing, and complete-tree escape validation remain open. Required sandbox mode remains fail-closed and unintegrated.
- The non-test desktop binary builds successfully with the timeout/cancellation contract, and `git diff --check` is clean apart from Git's existing CRLF normalization notice for the roadmap.
## Windows AppContainer stdio capture — 2026-10-21

- Added explicit stdin/stdout/stderr pipes to the native AppContainer launcher. Because Windows rejects `PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST` together with AppContainer security capabilities, the single-use helper temporarily disables inheritance on its protocol standard handles while creating the child; only the three dedicated stdio pipe handles are left inheritable. Stdin closes after forwarding, and stdout/stderr drain concurrently with independent 1 MiB retained-output caps while discarding overflow to avoid pipe deadlocks.
- Added tests for stdout/stderr separation and output exceeding the cap. With native Windows test permissions, `cargo test --locked --manifest-path gui/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml` passed twice (18/18 each run), including the ACL recovery cases.
- The Rust command outcome now carries raw output bytes and truncation flags. Node/Tauri process/runtime integration, cancellation wiring, trusted helper packaging, Linux-host validation, and full sandbox security validation remain open; required mode remains unavailable.

## Persistent goal progress and references — 2026-10-21

## Deterministic end-to-end agent evaluation — 2026-10-21

- Added a versioned offline scenario runner that executes the real AgentRuntime with deterministic OpenAI-compatible fixtures and isolated temporary workspaces. It checks completion status, selected model, response predicates, and expected tool execution order; reports model/tool calls, denials, token usage, estimated cost, latency, and aggregate task success; and excludes prompts and response text from reports.
- The offline runner uses deterministic response fixtures, runs in Windows/Linux CI, and requires no live model or network service. It now covers six scenarios, including approved and denied edits with exact workspace-file assertions. `pnpm eval:agent` passes all 6 scenarios. Broader plan/review/multi-file/adversarial scenarios, calibrated policy-violation scoring, and representative task labels remain open.

## Worktree lease recovery — 2026-10-21

- New worktree lease records include a random token and captured OS process identity. `worktrees locks` reports active, stale, PID-reused, remote, and unverifiable owners without changing files. `worktrees recover-lock <id>` is explicit and removes only a same-host lease after identity proves the recorded process is absent or its PID now names a different process; it rechecks file identity and token. Lease release also preserves a replacement lock file.
- Focused Windows worktree tests pass 4/4, including a real Git lifecycle, active-owner refusal, stale-owner recovery, and replacement-lock preservation. `pnpm lint` and formatting pass. Two full project checks pass (348 tests each: 343 passed, 5 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 122 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Linux process-identity and Bubblewrap integration remain required CI evidence.

## Subagent crash and slot recovery — 2026-10-21

- Subagent task records and concurrency slots now store bounded OS process identities and random lease tokens. Read-only inspection classifies active, interrupted, and unverifiable owners; explicit recovery rechecks owner and slot identities, releases only the task's stale slot and worktree lease, marks the task failed with a safe interruption reason, and preserves its worktree for review. Mutation-lock acquisition also reclaims a same-host lock only after verifying its owner exited; slot, mutation, and normal lease release paths compare file identity/token before unlinking.
- Tests cover automatic stale mutation-lock recovery, a real child process force-killed while holding a subagent task and slot, slot reuse after recovery, and existing failure/timeout behavior. Focused subagent tests pass 7/7. Two full project checks pass (350 tests each: 345 passed, 5 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 122 JavaScript modules syntax-checked); formatting, lint, and whitespace checks pass.

Configuration, credentials, approvals/grants, audit/usage, processes, worktrees, semantic indexes, and observability records are explicitly excluded.

## Lifecycle hooks — 2026-10-21

- Added opt-in user-configured hooks around model and tool calls, plus an error hook. Commands use direct argv execution, bounded JSON stdin and process output, and a configurable timeout (up to 10 seconds). Required sandbox mode routes hooks through the existing platform isolation layer and fails closed if isolation is unavailable. Assisted mode strips secret-like environment variables; unrestricted mode inherits the full user environment.
- Pre-model and pre-tool failures block the associated action and emit metadata-only `hook_failed` events. Post hooks and error hooks are advisory. Workspace config cannot install hooks, and hook stdout/stderr is not sent to the model. Hook scripts execute with the user's OS privileges and must be trusted.
- Configuration, failure behavior, sandbox routing, safe environment, and event contract tests pass. Two full project checks pass (364 tests each: 359 passed, 5 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 126 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm lint`, and `git diff --check` pass; lint retains eight existing informational notices. OS-enforced memory/CPU quotas and broader sandbox escape testing remain open.

## Signed extension package verification — 2026-10-21

- Added a version-1 directory package manifest and Ed25519 signatures over canonical metadata and every payload path, byte count, and SHA-256 digest. Verification rejects unknown/revoked signers and revisions, tampered or undeclared files, symlinks, traversal, Windows reserved names, portable path collisions, malformed versions, and oversized packages. Reads remain size-bounded if a file changes while being inspected.
- Added user-configured trust roots and local revocation entries; project config cannot add either. `extensions fingerprint`, `extensions verify`, and `extensions install` provide an out-of-band trust check and stage already verified bytes into version-addressed directories. Signed skills can be explicitly enabled, disabled, or rolled back with the CLI; runtime discovery re-verifies package signatures/revocations and still loads the content as untrusted instructions. MCP packages remain inert pending a user-configured server command. CLI and crypto tests cover trust, revocation, tampering, path validation, staging, activation, rollback, runtime loading, and config boundaries.
- Two full project checks pass after activation integration (372 tests each: 367 passed, 5 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 129 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Tests cover version switching, rollback, revocation rejection, runtime package re-verification, untrusted skill loading, and the CLI flow. `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm lint`, and `git diff --check` pass; lint retains eight existing informational notices. Signer distribution, authenticated remote revocation, and MCP package activation remain open.

## Signed revocation feed — 2026-10-21

- Added an optional HTTPS revocation feed signed by a configured Ed25519 trust key. `extensions refresh-revocations` bounds response bytes and time, rejects redirects, verifies timestamps/signature/schema, and enforces monotonically increasing sequence numbers to reject rollback or equivocation. Cache reads are no-follow, size-bounded, signature-verified, and expiry-checked.
- Runtime and package CLI verification merge cached remote revocations with local deny lists. Runtime never contacts the network and fails closed for active skills when configured data is missing, invalid, or expired. Refresh is explicit; with no feed configured, behavior remains fully local.
- Two full `pnpm test` runs pass on Windows (377 tests: 372 passed, 5 platform/capability skips, zero failures). Focused package/config/feed tests pass. `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm lint`, and `git diff --check` pass; lint retains eight existing informational notices. The package registry still lacks authenticated automatic package updates and MCP package activation.

## Authenticated package update metadata — 2026-10-21

- Added an optional Ed25519-signed package update feed with bounded HTTPS fetching, trusted-key and local-revocation checks, expiry, replay protection, and a no-follow local cache. `extensions check-updates` reports newer versions for active installed packages; `extensions list-updates` checks the cache offline while it remains valid. `extensions verify-update` requires the candidate package signature and its exact manifest-byte SHA-256 digest to match the cached signed feed.
- Verification and install remain separate; install verifies the full tree and does not activate or execute content. Automatic download, staged rollout, dependency resolution, application self-update, and MCP activation remain open.
- Two full test runs pass on Windows (380 tests: 375 passed, 5 platform/capability skips, zero failures). Focused package-update, extension CLI, and config tests pass. Formatting and changed-file lint pass; repository lint retains eight existing informational notices.

## Verified package update downloads — 2026-10-21

- Added a bounded JSON bundle format and `extensions install-update <type> <id>`. The client fetches only HTTPS URLs from a fresh verified update feed with redirects disabled and response/time limits. It decodes the bundle in memory, verifies the feed-listed raw manifest digest, publisher signature, identity, complete declared file set, and every file hash, then writes verified bytes to an immutable version directory. It never extracts an archive or auto-activates a skill/MCP package.
- Added publisher-side bundle creation and download/install tests for tampered payloads, incorrect manifest digests, multi-file packages, redirect policy, inactive staging, and the invariant that no active skill appears after download. Two full Windows test runs pass (381 tests each: 376 passed, 5 platform/capability skips, zero failures). Formatting and lint checks pass; lint reports eight informational notices. `git diff --check` passes. Linux and GUI integration coverage remains on the roadmap.

## Signed feed concurrency revalidation — 2026-10-21

- Closed a cross-process replay race in package-update and revocation feed cache commits. Concurrent fetches now acquire a bounded exclusive commit lock, reload and revalidate the latest signed cache, and enforce monotonic sequence/digest checks immediately before atomic replacement. A stuck lock fails closed and requires operator recovery after verifying no live writer.
- Added controlled concurrency regressions proving a late older response cannot replace the newer sequence for either feed. Focused update-feed and revocation suites passed 6/6 each.
- Two consecutive full Windows checks passed: each ran 422 tests (417 passed, 5 skipped, zero failures) and syntax-checked 143 JavaScript modules. Skips: two symlink creation restrictions (EPERM), Linux-only pidfd and Bubblewrap integrations, and the Windows-inapplicable process signal-timeout integration.
