## Signed package capability declarations — 2026-10-21

- Added optional capability_schema 1 and capabilities fields to signed package statements. Existing schema-1 packages without either field remain valid. Validation bounds declarations to 32 entries; checks exact record shape, enumerated capability names/scopes, duplicate name/scope pairs, canonical HTTPS or loopback HTTP origins, printable bounded reasons, and forbids executable requests from instruction-only skills. Capabilities are normalized for canonical signing.
- CLI verify/install output exposes declared capabilities and schema version; installation still returns enabled=false. Added coverage for all capability types, legacy verification, unsupported/partial schemas, altered signed scopes, wildcard/malformed scopes, duplicates, type mismatch, bidi controls, and CLI visibility.
- Focused package/update/revocation suites passed 21/21, plus the signed-package CLI integration passed 1/1. Two consecutive full Windows checks passed: each ran 429 tests (424 passed, 5 skipped, zero failures) and syntax-checked 145 modules. Formatting, lint, VS Code package checks, and whitespace validation passed; lint reports eight informational suggestions.
- Runtime enforcement remains a separate release gate. An installed MCP package has no signed launch-entry-point binding and stays inert; an independently configured MCP server is governed only by the user's MCP configuration and runtime approval/autonomy policy. Its capability claims cannot authorize or constrain that separate command. Documentation now states this boundary explicitly.

## Signed MCP entry-point binding — 2026-10-21

- Added an optional `entry_point` to the signed package statement for MCP packages. It must name an included `.mjs` payload and requires a signed `process.execute` declaration with `configured` scope. The entry point is covered by canonical Ed25519 signing and tampering, omission, traversal, wrong-type, and missing-capability cases are tested. Existing skill and MCP manifest formats without the field remain valid.
- Updated the extension guide, threat model, and Phase 5 roadmap to distinguish signed launch-target integrity from execution authorization. Package execution remains inert and runtime enforcement is still open.
- Focused package/update/revocation tests passed 22/22, and the CLI signed-package flow passed 1/1. Two consecutive full Windows checks passed (430 tests each: 422 passed, 8 platform skips, zero failures; 145 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Formatting, lint, VS Code package, whitespace, and deterministic source-release checks passed; lint reports eight existing informational notices. Native Linux package execution and runtime capability enforcement remain open.

## Signed MCP package activation — 2026-10-21

- Added explicit enable, disable, and rollback for signed MCP packages using the existing versioned extension registry. Activation and rollback verify publisher trust, local revocation policy, and the signed entry-point/process declaration. Runtime discovery rechecks active MCP signatures, payload digests, and cached revocations, with a 16-package activation cap.
- User MCP servers can reference an activated package with `package_id`; command and cwd overrides are rejected. CLI, API, and agent startup pass the same user trust/revocation configuration into the MCP manager. Startup launches only the signed `.mjs` entry point, requires declarations for advertised tools/resources/prompts, and adds Linux Bubblewrap isolation for required-mode stdio MCP. Added registry, tampering, revocation, config, CLI, and real child-process MCP integration coverage.
- Signed MCP packages still execute with the current OS account's direct filesystem/network/desktop permissions. The declarations currently gate only advertised MCP feature categories; persistent-process OS isolation and fine-grained capability enforcement remain open.
- Focused config, package, and MCP suites passed 32/32; CLI package activation passed 1/1. Two consecutive full Windows checks passed (433 tests each: 425 passed, 8 platform skips, zero failures; 145 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Linux-native MCP and sandbox validation remain open.
- Live LM Studio integration was also verified on `127.0.0.1:1234`: model discovery returned the server catalog, the product CLI completed a fixed-prompt chat, a minimal OpenAI-compatible function-call request returned a tool call, and a CLI agent run executed `run_process` in a temporary workspace and returned its verified output. The temporary test workspace was removed afterward.

## Linux Bubblewrap isolation for MCP stdio — 2026-10-21

- Required Linux mode now launches persistent MCP stdio children inside Bubblewrap with network, IPC, and PID namespaces; all capabilities dropped; cleared minimal environment; read-only mounts for the resolved runtime and signed package; and only the selected workspace writable. The package is mounted by canonical verified path, and the sandbox refuses configured environment references and HTTP MCP. Windows persistent MCP remains fail-closed.
- Code review tightened read-only mount target validation and removed inherited host environment variables from the Bubblewrap launcher, including Node preload and dynamic loader injection variables. Added overlapping-mount and fixed-launch-environment regressions plus a Linux integration test that CI requires when its Bubblewrap fixture is installed.
- Two final-state full project checks pass (436 each: 427 passed, 9 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 145 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Focused sandbox/MCP tests pass (16 passed, 1 Linux-only skip); formatting, lint, VS Code package validation, and whitespace checks pass. Eight lint informational notices remain. Two final release builds are byte-identical. Native Linux Bubblewrap execution remains unverified locally because WSL is not installed; independent sandbox escape testing and Windows persistent MCP isolation remain open.

## File-tool read identity pinning — 2026-10-22

- `read_file` opens the resolved target once, uses that handle for metadata and streaming, rejects final-component symlinks where `O_NOFOLLOW` is supported, and compares the opened device/inode with a fresh path stat after re-resolving the path. Writes and edits now re-resolve the user's requested path immediately before atomic replacement and fail when it no longer resolves to the initially selected target; a regression simulates a target swap and confirms neither file changes.
- The focused file-tool suite passes (12 passed, one Windows symlink-capability skip). Two full Windows suites pass (552 tests each: 535 passed, 17 platform/capability skips, zero failures); `pnpm check` syntax-checked 174 JavaScript modules. Formatting, lint, and `git diff --check` pass; lint reports six informational notices. Parent-directory swaps and replacement races after the final path check remain open; portable Node path APIs do not provide race-free openat-style traversal, so this does not close the TOCTOU gate.
## User-selectable application update controls — 2026-10-22

- Revalidated the implemented update boundary: Runtime settings offer Never check, manual-only checks, or startup notification, with Stable/Preview channels. The signed artifact download requires a fresh check and explicit button action; installation stays a user-controlled Windows installer/Linux package-manager step.
- Application update tests pass twice (10/10 each). The headless Edge UI smoke passes twice and confirms all three modes, both channels, the check button, accessible controls, and keyboard navigation. A first sandboxed attempt could not reach loopback; both authorized local-service runs passed.
## Tool policy revalidation at execution — 2026-10-22

- AgentRuntime now reloads the effective policy after any approval wait and immediately after required `pre_tool` hooks, before calling the tool. A newly added deny rule prevents execution; if an action that was previously allowed now requires approval, execution is refused and the model must propose it again. Exact-action session grants are reloaded at both checks; a revoked or expired grant cannot authorize execution. If an external grant change advanced the session revision, the runtime rebases only when all non-grant session fields still match.
- Expiry regressions now exercise all persistent approval-rule scopes (tool, canonical path, argv command), verifying active allow/deny behavior and that expired rules no longer affect decisions. An AgentRuntime test expires an exact-action session grant from a `pre_tool` hook and confirms the write is denied before execution. Focused Windows validation passed: 83 tests across the agent and approval-rule-store suites. Final-tree Windows verification passed twice: 607 total tests per run (589 passed, 18 platform/capability skips, zero failures), 175 JavaScript modules syntax-checked, lint/format/whitespace checks pass, the VS Code package check passes, and the real-browser approval smoke verifies an approved reviewed hunk is written.
- Agent tests pass 71/71, covering policy changes during approval and pre-tool hooks plus grant revocation during a pre-tool hook. Two full Windows checks pass (555 tests each: 538 passed, 17 platform/capability skips, zero failures); `pnpm check` syntax-checked 174 JavaScript modules. Format, lint, and whitespace checks pass; lint has six informational notices. Linux-native sandbox, process, and package tests remain unverified here.
# Semantic embedding usage and shared budgets — 2026-10-22

- Semantic embedding requests now reserve estimated input tokens before sending and settle them through the shared daily/monthly usage ledger. The OpenAI-compatible adapter uses valid provider `usage.prompt_tokens` when present and records a text-length estimate otherwise; records carry model and session attribution when available.
- Agent automatic retrieval, the approved semantic tool, authenticated API search/reindex, and CLI index operations all share the ledger. Unrestricted agent mode continues to record usage while bypassing configured shared hard limits, matching its other application-level budget behavior.
- Added optional `[retrieval.semantic].input_cost_per_million`. Configured prices include embeddings in cost caps; unknown prices are recorded as unknown and fail closed before sending when a cost cap is active. Documentation describes this behavior.
- Focused embedding, semantic-index, config, and usage-ledger tests pass 34/34. Two final full Windows checks each passed 566 tests (549 passed, 17 platform/capability skips, zero failures) and syntax-checked 174 JavaScript modules. Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass. Native Linux validation, usage-estimator calibration, embedding latency measurement, and extension usage accounting remain open.

## Reproducible retrieval scale benchmark and workspace batching — 2026-10-22

- Added `pnpm bench:retrieval` with bounded synthetic-workspace inputs and JSON measurements for generation time, initial index/search, warm search, embedding request counts, chunk batch size, index bytes, and RSS change. It makes no provider requests and cleans up its workspace.
- The initial measurement exposed a per-file batching defect: 1,500 files and 3,000 chunks caused one request per file. Semantic indexing now batches pending chunks across file boundaries, up to the provider limit of 32, without changing partial-index caps or index-write atomicity. A regression verifies 40 file chunks group into 32 and 8, followed by the query vector.
- On Windows x64 / Node 24.19.0, the 1,500-file, 2-KiB synthetic run discovered and indexed all files and 3,000 chunks; after file identity/path validation was added, indexing plus query took 1,898.64 ms, warm search took 1,736.63 ms, and the index file was 883,680 bytes. It made 94 chunk requests of at most 32 inputs and two query requests. These timings are host-specific and use a deterministic in-process embedding callback; native Linux and real repository/provider comparisons remain open.
- The latest scanner hardening is recorded below.

## Semantic scanner resists directory-swap escape — 2026-10-22

- The scanner now canonicalizes each traversed directory before and after enumeration, rechecks each candidate file's real path, requires a regular non-symlink file, opens with no-follow where available, compares device/inode/size/mtime through the opened handle, and reads at most the configured file-byte ceiling. A deterministic filesystem seam swaps an enumerated workspace directory to an outside junction/symlink; the regression proves the outside canary never reaches the embedding callback.
- The focused semantic suite passed 9/9 with `XYST_REQUIRE_LINK_TESTS=true`, including the race fixture. Two consecutive capability-aware full Windows checks passed 568 tests each (551 passed, 17 platform/capability skips, zero failures), syntax-checked 175 modules, and were followed by clean lint, format, and whitespace checks. Forcing every link fixture on this host failed four unrelated audit/session link tests because those fixtures return `EPERM`; that host capability limitation remains visible, while the new scanner link test itself ran successfully when forced. Native Linux race validation and race-free `openat`-style traversal remain open.

## Shared budget settlement overruns and application update preferences — 2026-10-22

- Usage reservations now retain the shared daily/monthly token and cost limits that applied when the request started. Settlement writes the actual provider usage, releases the completed reservation, and then checks settled totals plus other in-flight reservations against those original limits. If actual usage exceeds the cap, the ledger keeps the usage and the agent stops with `budget_exceeded`; later reservations remain blocked until the accounting period permits them. Added regressions for token and cost overruns, concurrent requests, and agent stop behavior.
- Before settlement, persisted reservation limits are now checked for the exact supported fields and valid non-negative numeric values. A corrupted limit record fails closed with `BUDGET_LEDGER_INVALID`, leaving usage totals unchanged and the reservation available for inspection; the regression covers this recovery-safe behavior.
- Runtime settings continue to let users change application update preferences later: Never check, manual-only checks, or startup notification, plus Stable/Preview channels. Downloads require an explicit action after signed verification; installation remains a user-controlled step.
- Focused budget, agent, and application-update tests passed 93/93 before the ledger-integrity follow-up; the updated ledger suite then passed 10/10. Two consecutive full Windows checks after the follow-up each passed 572 tests (555 passed, 17 platform/capability skips, zero failures) and syntax-checked 175 JavaScript modules. Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass. Linux-native validation, production release infrastructure, independent security review, and remaining roadmap items are still open.

## Linux Bubblewrap workspace root swap guard — 2026-10-22

- Required Bubblewrap launches now carry the device/inode identity captured during workspace validation into the sandbox and compare it with the actual `/workspace` mount before executing the requested command. This protects one-shot commands, managed background processes, and stdio MCP processes from a workspace-root replacement between validation and mount; a mismatch exits before the workload runs. The check uses the system `/usr/bin/stat` inside the read-only system mount and fails closed if it is unavailable.
- Added an Ubuntu-only integration case that validates a workspace, replaces its root path with a different directory, then verifies Bubblewrap refuses to run a marker-writing command. Unit coverage verifies the guard arguments and captured identity. This Windows host cannot execute the Linux Bubblewrap test; Ubuntu CI sets `XYST_REQUIRE_BWRAP_TEST=true` so the integration cannot silently skip there. Intra-workspace mutation races and broader sandbox escape validation remain open.
- Windows sandbox, background-process, and MCP-focused coverage passed (32 passed, 3 platform/capability skips); the Windows AppContainer integration passed separately with host ACL access. Two full Windows checks each passed 573 tests (556 passed, 17 platform/capability skips, zero failures) and syntax-checked 175 JavaScript modules. Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass. The new real Bubblewrap root-swap case still needs execution in Ubuntu CI.

## Read-only MCP catalog in browser and API — 2026-10-22

- Added an authenticated `/v1/mcp/servers` endpoint and browser panel for configured server state and advertised tool/resource/prompt names. The authenticated catalog and browser panel display bounded error codes and never return raw process configuration or error text. Manager and API regressions cover mixed healthy/failing servers and catalog availability after startup failures. Dynamic enable/disable/removal controls and their verification are recorded in the follow-up below; adding servers and changing connection details still require editing user configuration.

Configuration writes are bounded and schema-validated; credentials must be environment or OS-vault references. The explicit connection test discovers advertised capabilities in a separate process and closes it without exposing tools to agent runs. Removal uses a persisted tombstone so the original TOML stays intact; the server can be added again through the panel or API. ## Provider failure classification — 2026-10-22

- Added a closed `ProviderErrorCode` union covering the codes emitted by the OpenAI-compatible provider. Chat requests classify HTTP 413 and explicit HTTP 400 context-length responses as `CONTEXT_OVERFLOW`; HTTP 502, 503, and 504 map to `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`. Context-message detection is limited to chat requests so model-list errors are not mislabeled. Configured fallback chains now retry another eligible model after `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` while preserving cancellation and budget behavior.
- Provider tests cover explicit context overflow, unavailable gateway retryability, and non-chat classification. Agent integration verifies 503 fallback, larger-context fallback after a provider rejection, and a no-fallback `context_overflow` stop that releases all reserved input/output usage. Focused tests pass (provider 25/25; fallback and context-overflow cases 3/3). Final full Windows checks are recorded in the tool-call ID entry below. Linux-native provider/network behavior and the broader runtime error taxonomy remain open.

## Control API terminal-event validation — 2026-10-22

- Added a shared validator for `api_run_finished` and `api_run_failed` payloads. The server validates runtime completion status and session identity before publishing a result, bounds failure code/message lengths, and emits a controlled failure when a runtime result is malformed. The API client rejects malformed terminal payloads as `INVALID_STREAM` rather than handing them to UI consumers. The API reference documents the event fields and bounds.
- Focused terminal-event and client tests pass 45/45; Control API integration tests pass 35/35. Two full Windows checks pass (621 tests each: 603 passed, 18 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 181 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass. ## Assisted child environment filtering — 2026-10-22

- Closed a process-environment gap: Assisted child processes now omit SSH-agent and askpass handles, GPG agent state, credential-provider configuration pointers, and runtime code-injection variables such as `NODE_OPTIONS`, `PYTHONPATH`, shell startup hooks, and dynamic-library preload paths. Existing credential-name filtering and ordinary environment values remain. Unrestricted background processes continue to inherit the full user environment.
- Process and background-process tests pass 25/25 with one expected Windows signal-semantics skip. A spawned Node child confirms code-injection and SSH-agent variables are absent while a normal value survives; the unrestricted inheritance regression also passes. Two full Windows checks pass (622 tests each: 604 passed, 18 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 181 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass. Linux-native and supported-edition validation remain open.

- Follow-up review closed Git's numbered `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` / `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_n` / `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_n` override path by filtering all `GIT_CONFIG_*` variables in Assisted child environments. Process regressions pass 17/17 with one expected Windows signal-semantics skip. Two full Windows checks pass (622 tests each: 604 passed, 18 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 181 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass.

## Strict UTF-8 file reads — 2026-10-22

- `read_file` now uses a streaming fatal UTF-8 decoder, preserving a leading BOM and existing CRLF normalization while rejecting malformed byte sequences instead of substituting replacement characters. Regression coverage includes invalid UTF-8 and a multibyte Unicode character split across the 64 KiB stream boundary. The focused file-tool suite passes 14 tests with one expected Windows symlink-creation skip.
- Two full Windows checks pass (622 tests each: 604 passed, 18 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 181 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass. Native Linux and reparse-point coverage remain open.

## Strict, race-checked file edits — 2026-10-22

- `edit_file` now reads through one opened file handle with no-follow semantics where supported, checks file identity and the configured read-byte limit, rejects malformed UTF-8 and binary NUL bytes, and verifies the source hash again immediately before replacement. If another writer changes the source after the edit reads it, the operation returns `STALE_FILE` and preserves the external update. The tool tests cover invalid encoding preservation and an induced stale-source race.
- Focused tool/preview coverage passes 20 tests with one expected Windows symlink-capability skip. Two captured full Windows checks pass consecutively (623 tests each: 605 passed, 18 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 181 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). One earlier un-captured full run reported a single failure; it did not reproduce in either captured run. Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass. Native Linux/reparse-point and directory-handle race validation remain open.

## Approval-preview file identity checks — 2026-10-22

- File-change previews now open their target once with `O_NOFOLLOW` where supported, check the opened identity against the re-resolved target, and hash/read from the same handle. This prevents a parent-directory link swap from redirecting preview reads to an outside target. Large-file hashing is streamed from the pinned handle; text preview remains size-bounded and strict UTF-8. `assertFileChangeUnchanged` uses the same checked-handle path.
- Focused preview and tool suites pass 20/22 (two host symlink-capability skips). Two full Windows checks pass (624 tests each: 606 passed, 18 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 181 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass. Native Linux/reparse-point execution and residual races after the final write validation remain open.

## OpenAI-compatible stream termination — 2026-10-22

- Provider conformance now treats `[DONE]` as a valid terminal marker when a compatible local server omits `finish_reason`, inferring `tool_calls` when tool deltas exist and `stop` otherwise. A transport that closes without either a finish reason or `[DONE]` still fails as `INVALID_STREAM`. This preserves local OpenAI-compatible server behavior while retaining incomplete-close detection.
- Provider tests pass 26/26; the nine agent/API/CLI regressions exposed by the stricter trial behavior pass 9/9 after the compatibility fix. Two full Windows checks pass (625 tests each: 607 passed, 18 platform/capability skips, zero failures; 181 JavaScript modules syntax-checked). Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass. Native Linux provider-server validation remains open.

## Opt-in OTLP/HTTP JSON trace export — 2026-10-22

- Added a bounded OTLP/HTTP JSON exporter for correlated API, agent, model, tool, retrieval, memory, persistence, compaction, recovery, and subagent spans. It is off unless both the master observability switch and its user-configured OTLP switch are enabled. Remote HTTPS and loopback HTTP endpoints are accepted; redirects, URL credentials, literal header credentials, unsafe headers, workspace-level telemetry configuration, oversized batches/responses, missing or incorrect response content types, empty/malformed JSON, and invalid response shapes are rejected. Reference-only headers resolve from the environment or OS credential store. Transient failures get bounded retries with jitter; partial-success responses, including accepted-with-warning responses, are accounted for without retry. Exported attributes remain an explicit allowlist and omit prompts, paths, tool arguments/results, response text, and raw error messages; exported spans carry the sampled trace flag.

## Legacy MCP Streamable HTTP session lifecycle — 2026-10-22

- Added stateful-session compatibility for legacy MCP Streamable HTTP handshakes. The client validates server-issued `Mcp-Session-Id` values, retains them only in process memory, includes them in later endpoint-bound requests, starts a fresh handshake and retries once after an expired-session 404, coalesces concurrent session recovery, and sends a bounded best-effort DELETE at close. The current `2026-07-28` protocol remains stateless and does not propagate session IDs.

Hosted CI, native Ubuntu Desktop session and multi-monitor/accessibility validation, a clean-machine upgrade/rollback matrix, Windows AppContainer and Linux Bubblewrap escape/recovery matrices, WMI/out-of-band process attribution, protected release-signing operations, and independent security/legal review remain open. See the ordered gates and exact residual-risk descriptions in the roadmap and [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md).


## Nested Windows process recovery coverage — 2026-10-23

- Expanded the Windows recovered-stop regression to create a detached child and a nested grandchild, simulate manager identity loss, recover the persisted process record, and verify both descendants terminate. The focused regression passes twice; a full Windows `pnpm check` after the change passes 713 tests with 19 expected platform/capability skips, zero failures, and syntax-checks 205 JavaScript modules.
- Lint, format, and `git diff --check` pass. This strengthens ancestry-based Job Object recovery evidence; it does not solve out-of-tree WMI process attribution, physical Linux crash recovery, hosted CI, or the independent review gates.


## Linux manager-crash process recovery — 2026-10-23

- Added a Linux integration that starts a separate manager process, waits until both manager and managed-process identities are persisted, force-kills the manager, reopens the store in a fresh manager, and stops the exact detached process group through the pidfd-verified recovery path. It confirms the recovered session record reaches `exited` and the original process identity is absent or changed.
- The focused Linux integration passes twice. Two full Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 `pnpm check:linux` runs pass 729 tests each (four Windows-only skips, zero failures). Two full Windows `pnpm check` runs pass 713 each (20 expected platform/capability skips, zero failures); the Linux-only recovery integration is explicitly skipped there. Lint, format, and whitespace checks pass.
- Physical Ubuntu Desktop recovery validation, WMI/out-of-tree process attribution, hosted CI, and independent security review remain open; ordinary nested Windows process creation during Job Object attachment now has an integration regression.

## Windows WMI out-of-tree recovery boundary — 2026-10-23

- Added a native Windows integration that starts a bounded heartbeat process through `Win32_Process.Create`, captures its OS identity, simulates loss of the Xyst process manager, and recovers the tracked process tree. The test proves the WMI-created process is not parented under the tracked root, remains alive and writing after managed-tree recovery, and is terminated during test cleanup only through its captured identity. Cleanup verifies the process identity is gone before deleting the fixture.
- Managed background-process recovery explicitly does not attribute WMI/COM-created out-of-tree processes to a Xyst task. Windows CI requires the integration with `XYST_REQUIRE_WMI_TEST=true`; local accounts denied CIM process creation report a clear capability skip, while strict mode fails instead of silently skipping.
- Code review also caught and fixed a stale routing test expectation for the `automatic_routing_enabled` decision factor.
- Verification: two consecutive strict Windows `pnpm check` runs pass 718/738 tests (20 named platform/capability skips, zero failures) and syntax-check 207 JavaScript modules; both execute the required WMI integration. Two consecutive Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 `pnpm check:linux` runs pass 733/738 tests (five Windows-only skips, zero failures), with Bubblewrap, pidfd, filesystem-link, and Secret Service tests required and executed. The strict WMI integration passes 1/1; the local non-elevated capability path reports one explicit skip. `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`, and `git diff --check` pass. Physical Ubuntu Desktop validation, hosted CI execution, and independent security review remain open.

## AppContainer host-process access check — 2026-10-23

- Extended the native Windows helper integration to launch a separate same-user sibling process and, from inside the AppContainer, request `PROCESS_VM_READ` handles to both that sibling and the host test process. Both opens are denied. The fixture sibling is terminated during teardown, and strict Windows CI already requires the packaged-helper integration.
- Verification: two consecutive Windows `pnpm check` runs pass 718/738 tests (20 named platform/capability skips, zero failures) with both `XYST_REQUIRE_WINDOWS_SANDBOX_TEST=true` and `XYST_REQUIRE_WMI_TEST=true`; the real helper and WMI integrations execute. Two consecutive Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 `pnpm check:linux` runs pass 733/738 tests (five Windows-only skips, zero failures), including required Bubblewrap, pidfd, filesystem-link, and Secret Service integrations. Lint, formatting, whitespace, desktop license, Node runtime, Rust formatting, and Rust check pass. This closes only the tested process-memory handle path; junction/reparse escapes, the full DACL/ACL recovery matrix, broader object access, native Ubuntu Desktop, hosted CI, and independent security review remain open.

The test runs in the existing Windows desktop-host CI test step.

Rust formatting passes. Symlink and alternate reparse tags, live ACL mutation races, hosted CI, physical Linux desktop, and independent security review remain open.

## Expanded Windows AppContainer process-rights denial check — 2026-10-23

- Expanded the required AppContainer helper integration from a single process-memory read probe to individually request twelve sensitive process-object rights against both the host test process and an unrelated same-user sibling: terminate, create thread, VM operation/read/write, duplicate handle, create process, set quota/information, suspend/resume, write DACL, and write owner. The test closes any unexpectedly granted handle and reports the exact target/right pair before failing.
- The focused native integration passed twice. Two consecutive strict Windows full checks passed after the change: each passed 718/738 tests (20 expected platform/capability skips, zero failures; 207 JavaScript modules syntax-checked), with required WMI and AppContainer integrations executed. After syncing the test into the WSL ext4 checkout, `pnpm check:linux` passed with 733/738 tests (five expected Windows-only skips, zero failures; 207 modules syntax-checked). Lint, format, JavaScript syntax, and whitespace checks pass. Broader process-object types, process creation behavior, network controls, alternative reparse tags, and the complete recovery/ACL matrix remain open.
## Windows sandbox ACL recovery retry boundary — 2026-10-23

- Added a native regression that simulates interruption after the workspace DACL and inherited file permissions have been restored but while the recovery journal is still present. It reruns recovery twice, verifies original ACL bytes remain intact, and confirms the completed record is removed. This exercises the idempotent cleanup boundary after ACL restoration.
- The focused regression passes, and the full native Windows desktop target passes twice (23/23 each). Rust formatting passes; strict Clippy passes for all desktop targets and the packaged sandbox-helper target. Other recovery journal write/commit/ACL mutation/profile deletion boundaries, alternate reparse tags, physical Ubuntu Desktop, hosted CI, and independent security review remain open.
## Windows recovery torn-journal cleanup — 2026-10-23

- Recovery now deletes only interrupted journal temp files whose name matches the generated dot-prefixed 32-hex-digit ID and `.tmp` suffix. It rejects matching non-regular/reparse objects and leaves unrelated or malformed temp filenames untouched. This is safe before any ACL grant because the journal rename is the commit point and occurs before workspace permission changes.
- The regression verifies partial generated temp removal and preservation of both an unrelated temp file and a malformed-name temp file. The focused native test passes; two full Windows desktop test runs pass 24/24. Rust formatting and strict Clippy pass for desktop and helper targets, the packaged Windows helper was rebuilt, and its required AppContainer integration passes. Later journal commit and ACL/profile cleanup fault points remain open.
## Windows recovery journal write failure ownership — 2026-10-23

- Fixed a journal-write cleanup bug: a failed create-new could previously enter generic cleanup and delete a colliding temp path that this write attempt had not created. The writer now tracks temp-file ownership and removes a temp path only after this attempt successfully created it. Its deterministic internal ID seam validates the generated 128-bit hexadecimal format before constructing paths.
- Added regressions proving a pre-existing temp collision remains byte-for-byte intact and a failed atomic rename removes only the newly created temp file while preserving the existing journal destination. The full native Windows desktop suite passes twice (25/25 each). Strict Clippy, Rust formatting, and the rebuilt helper’s AppContainer integration pass. Further crash injection around journal flush/rename, profile deletion, and ACL transitions remains open.
## Windows recovery after profile deletion — 2026-10-23

- Added a native fault-state regression that restores the workspace ACL, deletes the AppContainer profile while leaving the recovery journal intact, then invokes recovery. Recovery re-derives and validates the profile SID, tolerates the already-deleted profile, preserves root and inherited file ACLs, and removes the completed journal.
- The focused native regression passes; the full Windows desktop test target passes twice (26/26 each). Rust formatting and strict Clippy pass for desktop and helper targets. The packaged helper was rebuilt from current source and its strict AppContainer integration passes. Physical Ubuntu Desktop, hosted CI, independent security review, later durability fault injection, and remaining escape matrices are still open.
## Windows recovery LOCALAPPDATA reparse-point rejection — 2026-10-23

- Fixed recovery setup validation that canonicalized LOCALAPPDATA before checking reparse attributes, allowing a final-component junction to be followed. It now requires an absolute path and checks each existing directory component, including ancestors, for reparse points before canonicalizing or creating recovery state.
- Added native regressions for both a junction used as LOCALAPPDATA and a normal child directory reached through a junctioned parent. Both verify recovery rejects the path and creates no state in the junction target. The full Windows native desktop target passes twice (28/28 each). Formatting and strict Clippy pass; the packaged helper was rebuilt and its AppContainer integration passes. Workspace symlink and alternate reparse tags, remaining crash boundaries, physical Ubuntu Desktop, hosted CI, and independent security review remain open.
## Windows workspace symbolic-link preflight coverage — 2026-10-23

- Added native file- and directory-symbolic-link fixtures. Both create an outside canary, require the preflight to reject the link as a reparse point, verify the canary remains unchanged, and remove the link without traversing it. Ordinary local attempts correctly returned Windows “insufficient privilege.”
- Marked the capability-dependent Rust tests ignored in ordinary local runs and added a dedicated Windows CI step using `--ignored`; it must execute both tests and will fail if the runner cannot create links. The command’s `--list` output confirms it selects exactly the two symbolic-link fixtures. Both fixtures also passed locally when the test executable was explicitly launched through UAC. Hosted CI has not yet been observed.
- The ordinary Windows native target passes twice (28 passed, two capability-dependent tests ignored, zero failures). The directory-junction regression, Windows AppContainer integration, strict Clippy, Rust formatting, Biome lint/format, and whitespace checks pass.

## Child process early-stdin-close handling — 2026-10-25

- Fixed an unhandled `child.stdin` stream error that could terminate Xyst when a subprocess exited before consuming a large input payload. `runProcess` now attaches its error listener before writing and includes the observed `stdinErrorCode` in the process result so incomplete delivery is visible to the caller. The full process suite passes on Windows (18 passed, one expected Windows signal-model skip) and Ubuntu WSL (19/19); Biome and whitespace checks pass.
- The process progress summary now surfaces only a validated stdin error code (without copying process output). The process and tool-summary suites pass together on Windows (20 passed, one expected platform skip); Biome and whitespace checks pass.


## LM Studio automatic keyless lookup and credential UX — 2026-10-26

- Keyless Wikipedia search is enabled by default. Explicit current-information requests use a compact required-search call with only `web_search` exposed, a 512-token output cap, and a 30-second request limit; afterward the normal tool set is restored. Workspace-only wording does not trigger live search. Wikipedia results are enriched with bounded plain-text page extracts. Empty/denied searches fail as `LIVE_LOOKUP_REQUIRED` rather than presenting unverified current facts. Brave remains optional for broader coverage.
- The local sample profile no longer includes the placeholder `LOCAL_MODEL_API_KEY`. The model editor explains that credentials are optional and LM Studio normally needs none. LM Studio fallback profiles are checked and loaded through its management API when the model is installed but not loaded.
- A real saved-config live lookup through the configured Qwen 3.5 9B → GPT-OSS 20B → Qwen 3 4B fallback path returned the current Canadian prime minister, Mark Carney, with its Wikipedia source URL. LM Studio rejected the GPT-OSS load with HTTP 500; Xyst skipped it and continued with configured Qwen 3 4B.
- Final full checks each ran 883 tests and passed. Windows: **858 passed, zero failed, 25 platform/capability skips**. Ubuntu 24.04 WSL: **877 passed, zero failed, six Windows-only skips**. Both syntax-check 230 JavaScript modules. Mock local provider profiles now use a separate loopback port so the LM Studio management loader does not consume their mocked provider responses. Focused API reruns and the Windows process-tree integration also pass. Browser smoke passed earlier in this turn, and the real saved-config live lookup returned Mark Carney with its source URL. `git diff --check` passes with only Git line-ending normalization warnings.

## Local dependency and secret scan — 2026-10-26

- The CI-pinned `cargo-audit 0.22.2` scanned the 477-crate Rust lockfile. It found no vulnerability-severity advisories after Tokio was updated to 1.54.1, but initially reported unsound `glib 0.18.5` iterator code and four unmaintained crates. gtk-rs PR 2009 was closed without merging and no fixed registry release is available, so the exact GLib source was vendored and patched using the upstream mainline fix to pass `&mut p` as the C out-parameter. The package uses local compatibility version `0.18.6`; this is not claimed to be an upstream release. The optimized regression `test_variant_iter_array` passes twice (1/1 each) on Ubuntu WSL; the Linux Tauri app passes `cargo check --locked`, and all 11 Linux desktop Rust tests pass from an ext4 source copy.
- CI runs `cargo audit --deny unsound --ignore RUSTSEC-2024-0429`, with the exception documented for the locally patched GLib source. A fresh audit passes with four unmaintained warnings. Hosted CI and independent security review remain open.
- Gitleaks 8.30.0 was downloaded from the official release and checksum-verified against its published checksum file. A local redacted scan of 361 tracked and non-ignored workspace files found nine high-entropy UUID fixtures in `tests/sandbox.test.mjs`; these were replaced with a zero UUID test placeholder. The focused sandbox suite then passed 19 tests with two expected platform skips, and the same scan found zero secrets. This local scan does not replace hosted CI or an independent security review.

# Background-process interactive input error handling — 2026-10-26

- A code review found that `writeInput()` did not observe a child stdin error when `write()` returned `true` and the pipe failed asynchronously. It now waits for the write callback, handles stream errors, returns a fixed safe message plus a validated error code, and removes its temporary listener after settlement.
- Added a regression that simulates a late `EPIPE` after `write()` reports success and confirms the manager rejects safely, does not expose the child diagnostic, and leaves no error listener behind. `tests/background-processes.test.mjs` passed twice on Windows (9 passed, two expected platform/environment skips per run). The full Windows `pnpm check` also completed successfully with required WMI and AppContainer integrations enabled and all 232 JavaScript modules syntax-checked. Biome formatting, lint, and whitespace checks pass for the changed files.
- The full Linux background-process suite passed twice in Ubuntu 24.04 WSL (9 passed, two expected Windows-only skips per run), including the live pidfd recovery integration. The full Windows `pnpm check` also passed with required WMI and AppContainer integration checks enabled. Rebuilt the Windows NSIS package; its isolated install, runtime/API readiness, resize/workspace restore, reinstall/config retention, uninstall, and registry restoration smoke passed. Applied it to the existing user install and relaunched Xyst; the installed runtime file matches source byte-for-byte. Release installer SHA-256: `52c3d1b02e479217c6bf96d28e98bbdf786240869ab3badf8aefc536f540dc43`.
- Further review found process-state JSON loading followed links and had no byte limit. The manager now validates each `.agent/processes` directory component, opens records with `O_NOFOLLOW` where supported, rejects non-regular, multiply linked, identity-changing, or over-8-MiB files, and rechecks directory identity before writes. The complete suite passed twice on a disposable Ubuntu 24.04 ext4 copy (11 passed, two expected Windows-only skips per run), including symlinked directory/file, oversized record, and Linux pidfd recovery tests. Full Windows `pnpm check` passed **874/900** (zero failures; 26 skips) with required WMI/AppContainer integrations, and syntax-checked 232 JavaScript modules. Windows local symlink tests skip because this account receives `EPERM`; hosted Windows behavior remains to verify.
- Rebuilt the x64 NSIS package from the hardened source. Its full isolated install/API/runtime, resize/workspace restoration, reinstall/config retention, uninstall, and registry restoration smoke passed. Updated the existing per-user installation; the installed `background-processes.mjs` SHA-256 matches source and the existing `config.toml` hash remained unchanged. Relaunched Xyst and verified the window title and responsiveness. Current package: `dist/releases/Xyst AI Assistant_0.1.2_x64-setup.exe`, SHA-256 `92fa189feaa9dac727e282d80ae2a47b8f1ed6caa7ae1937c35a8775c6e7b88e`; the prior package is preserved in `dist/releases/archive/2026-10-26-pre-process-state-hardening/`.

## Approval persistence and API shutdown review — 2026-10-27

- Hardened approval-rule persistence: reject symlinked/non-directory state paths, reject non-regular or hard-linked/oversized state files, read through a no-follow handle with identity checks, and remove a lock only when its identity still matches. Added a regression proving a linked `.agent/approvals` directory cannot redirect writes outside the workspace.
- Local API shutdown now closes remaining HTTP connections after stopping active runs. Test cleanup also closes mock provider connections and stops interactive test processes even after assertion failures.
- Focused API, VS Code, and approval tests pass on Windows and Ubuntu 24.04 WSL (72/72 on each). Added independent rules-file and lock-file symlink, hard-link, and size-bound regressions; all 10 approval-store tests pass on Ubuntu ext4. The Windows account cannot create hard links, and the corresponding tests report explicit capability skips there. Biome lint, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- Full current-source suites now pass: Windows 881/909 (zero failures, 28 documented platform/capability skips, required WMI coverage) and Ubuntu 24.04 WSL ext4 903/909 (zero failures, six expected Windows-only skips); both syntax-check 234 JavaScript modules. The prior interrupted/timed-out runs are superseded by these clean results.
- The current Windows installed runtime's `approval-rule-store.mjs` and `api/server.mjs` match workspace hashes byte-for-byte. Current NSIS, DEB, and AppImage builds postdate those runtime sources and have passed their isolated lifecycle/package smokes; hosted CI and physical Ubuntu Desktop/Wayland validation remain outstanding.
- Live local inference confirms a model-specific reliability issue: the saved Qwen 3.5 9B profile responds to a tiny prompt in about 23 seconds but timed out after five minutes during the full file-tool smoke. The already-loaded Ministral 3 3B profile passed the same full file/process/shell smoke on a repeat run; one earlier run completed its tool actions but missed the exact final marker. No saved model setting was changed. Review Auto routing and user-facing model selection/performance guidance before claiming every configured local profile is reliable.

## Desktop proxy integration — 2026-10-27

- The desktop service now enables the bundled Node runtime's environment proxy handling when standard proxy variables are configured, preserves bypass entries, and forces local LM Studio/control endpoints to stay direct. `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=0` is an explicit opt-out.
- Windows desktop startup also reads the current user's enabled manual Internet Settings proxy, including separate HTTP/HTTPS servers and bypass entries, when environment variables do not specify that protocol. PAC/WPAD, `<local>` semantics, Linux desktop setting discovery, and proxy-authentication UI remain open. The registry parser is bounded and does not log proxy addresses or credentials.
- The environment-proxy integration test and Windows NSIS smoke passed before the Windows system-proxy discovery change; the refreshed NSIS installer was applied to the per-user installation and the user config hash stayed identical. The new registry integration awaits the final cross-platform test and packaging pass requested by the user.
